# Execution Tracker

> [!NOTE]
> ## Proposed restructure (from external review — not yet actioned)
>
> This file has grown large. A reviewer recommended splitting it into a
> `docs/tracking/` directory (`phase-0.md`, `phase-1a.md`, `phase-1b.md`,
> `phase-1c-1e.md`, `maintenance.md`, `deferred.md`) with this file kept as a
> short summary index linking to them. **Deferred** — it is a structural change
> to design and agree separately, not part of the status refresh.
>
> The review also flagged status values used below that are **outside the
> documented vocabulary** (see "Status Values"): `Accepted` (P1A-001, P1A-003),
> `Designed` (P1B-001), `Accepted (full scope)` (P1B-FONT), `Fixed by P1B-004`
> (P1B-R2), and `Planned` (P1C-003, P1D-003, P1E-001..003). These should either
> be mapped onto the vocabulary (e.g. Accepted/Designed → Ready or Done;
> Planned → Not started) or the vocabulary should be extended to admit them.

## Purpose

Records project execution state. Each entry is terse: id, title, status, and a
one-sentence outcome plus its design reference. Full detail lives in git history,
PRs, and the design docs — chiefly the consolidated `38-SCHEMA-V1-DESIGN-REFERENCE.md`
(referenced by anchor, e.g. `#tables`). Update this file when work begins,
changes scope, or finishes.

## Status Values

Not started · Researching · Designing · Finalizing · Ready · In progress ·
Blocked · In review · Done

| M-006 | Fix escapeHtml missing import in webapp main.js | Antigravity | Done | Added missing `escapeHtml` import from `./clipboard.mjs` in `webapp/src/main.js` which caused `ReferenceError` on startup when populating table styles. Unit tests pass cleanly. |
| M-007 | Harden `webapp/build.sh` against concurrent-worktree build races | Claude Code | Done | Concurrent agent builds intermittently failed at wasm-opt ("No such file or directory" / "Blocking waiting for file lock on build directory"). Two shared resources were racing: the cargo build-directory lock and the shared per-user `wasm-opt` download cache. Fix pins `CARGO_TARGET_DIR="$repo/target"` (per-worktree, so concurrent builds never share a build lock; CI keeps its repo-local `./target` that rust-cache keys on), serializes only the cold-cache `wasm-opt` download behind a portable `mkdir` lock (warm cache stays fully parallel), and adds a bounded jittered retry as a self-healing safety net. Verified: `build.sh` succeeds (wasm-opt ran, `pkg` emitted); two overlapping heavy builds on a shared target dir reproduce `Blocking waiting for file lock`, while isolated target dirs compile in parallel with no lock wait. No CI behaviour change. |

| MFIO-008 | ODT→DOCX fidelity parity | Claude Code | In progress | Goal: raise the ODT adapter to the DOCX adapter's fidelity (DOCX is full+symmetric across all 22 families; a dual-agent inventory produced the family-by-family gap). Prioritized: (T1) round-trip loss fixes — hyperlinks + bookmarks; (T2) run/paragraph/table properties; (T3) fields; (T4) named-style identity; (T5) sections/headers-footers/anchored images; (T6) comments, tracked changes, shapes/text-boxes, math, forms, TOC. **T1 done** (+reviewed): hyperlink (`text:a`, external + internal + `office:title`) and bookmark (`text:bookmark-start/-end`) export now round-trip to a byte-exact fixed point (were import-only, silently dropped on export); `xmlns:xlink` added to the semantic content header; export mirrors the importer's hyperlink-scheme allowlist and degrades unserializable/blocked values rather than aborting (review-found HIGH regression + MEDIUM security gap, both fixed). **T2a done** (+reviewed): run properties extended to font family, super/subscript, all-caps, small-caps on import + export (fixed a latent `merge_run_properties` bug dropping run properties outside the original 6-field subset; review caught a font-family export-abort + import-trim fixed-point break, both fixed). **T2b done**: paragraph properties — indentation (incl. hanging), spacing (incl. percent line-height), keep-with-next/keep-together/break-before — round-trip on import+export; the alignment-only automatic style is generalized to a deterministic `OdtParagraphStyle` keeping historical names; exactly-reversible `pt` lengths with `cm`/`mm`/`in` accepted on import. Tab stops + borders/shading remain reported remainders. **T2c(1) done**: table grid column widths round-trip via a new `table-column` style family (`style:column-width` ↔ `GridColumn.width_twips`), export emitting a deterministic per-width `table-column` automatic style and grouping equal consecutive columns; width-less tables stay byte-identical. **T2c(2) done**: table cell shading (`fo:background-color` → `Shading.fill`) and vertical alignment (`style:vertical-align` ↔ `CellVerticalAlignment`) round-trip via a parallel `table-cell` style family, reusing the `recognized`-attribute mechanism; property-less cells stay byte-identical. **T2c(3) done**: table cell borders — `fo:border` + per-edge `fo:border-*` (width/style/color) round-trip via the `table-cell` family with an exact eighth-point↔pt codec (domain-clamped `0..=1024`); four equal edges collapse to the shorthand; inside-H/V and padded edges reported. Row height round-trips via a new table-row family (style:row-height/min-row-height). Table-level width (style:width/rel-width) + alignment (table:align) round-trip via a new table style family. **T2c(6) done**: cell content margins (`fo:padding` + per-edge `fo:padding-*` ↔ `CellMargins`, domain-clamped `0..=31_680` twips, four equal edges collapse to the shorthand) — **the table family is now complete both directions** (structure, merges, column widths, cell shading/valign/borders/margins, row height, table width/align). **T3 done fields**: `text:page-number`/`page-count`/`date`/`time`/`bookmark-ref`(text+page reference)/`sequence` all round-trip to `FieldKind::Page`/`NumPages`/`Date`/`Time`/`Ref`/`PageRef`/`Seq` on import+export (inline field-capture via skip_active_subtree; cached display + unsupported formats dropped; a field inside a hyperlink degrades since the model forbids a field in an inline wrapper; control chars in a ref target/name round-trip as XML numeric refs). A latent `is_xml_character` bug (rejecting tab/LF/CR, corrupting attribute escaping since T1) was root-fixed. **T6(2) done TOC**: a block-level `text:table-of-content` ↔ `BlockNode::Sdt` (BuildingBlockGallery "Table of Contents", `text:name`→`tag`, index-body entries→blocks) via a splice mechanism (record the body-draft length at the open, splice the entries out at the matching End — no new block-container threading); the level-template source is dropped, an empty TOC is dropped (else EmptySdt aborts), and an unnamed TOC mints a document-unique `text:name` on export. Fields + TOC now all round-trip. **T6(1) done comments**: an inline `office:annotation` <-> `CommentReference` + `Comment` definition round-trips to a byte-exact fixed point — `dc:creator`→author, `dc:date`→date, `text:p`/`text:h` body flattened to a single plain-text paragraph (decoding the writer's own `text:s`/`text:tab`/`text:line-break` on re-import so whitespace survives), via a self-contained `parse_annotation` sub-parser (modeled on `parse_draw_frame`, off the main inline state machine) and an inline-scoped `xmlns:dc` so comment-free docs stay byte-identical; the paired `office:annotation-end` range and thread metadata are dropped (point comment). Remaining T6: tracked changes, shapes/text-boxes, math, forms; T4 named-style identity. A parallel design workflow produced adversarially-vetted first-slice scopes + a value×tractability roadmap for the remaining families. Each tier lands as an adversarially-reviewed slice. |
| MFIO-PDF | PDF export + print backend | Claude Code | Proposed | Design accepted-pending: `98-PDF-EXPORT-AND-PRINT-DESIGN.md` + **ADR-031**. New `casual-doc-pdf` backend transcribes the shared `DisplayList` (ADR-003) into a real, deterministic PDF — selectable text layer via `Type0`/CID fonts + `ToUnicode` from `Glyph.cluster`, embedded **subset** fonts (exact layout faces from `FontRegistry`, the editor↔PDF parity guarantee), vector paint mapping (rasterizer is the reference traversal), and image XObjects — **never** rasterized pages or outlined text. Editor↔PDF parity is structural (exporter does zero layout) + CI-enforced by a display-list geometry golden; Word parity is *feature* parity (not rendering). **Phasing:** Phase 0 = proper vector PDF (container, paint, **font subsetting = long pole**, images, wiring) ≈ 2–3.5 sprints (crate) / 3.5–5.5 (hand-rolled); Phase 1 = semantic features (StructureTree bridge, metadata, external+internal links, outline/bookmarks) ≈ 1.5–2.5 sprints; Print (PDF→browser dialog / host adapter) ≈ 0.5 sprint; Phase 2 (tagged/accessible PDF + PDF/A + encryption + markup) ≈ 3.5–6 sprints, **GATED** on an accessibility/archival requirement (≈ doubles the effort). **Ship-worthy target = Phase 0+1+Print ≈ 4–7 sprints (1 eng).** **Blocked on ADR-031** (writer/subsetter build-vs-buy vs `deny.toml`/`unsafe_code=forbid`) + the Phase-2 in/out gate. Estimates ±40%, exclude layout-engine Word-fidelity work and font licensing. |

## Phase 1B — Semantic DOCX writer (model -> WordprocessingML)

**Status: Complete** (accepted 2026-07-26, `41-PHASE-1B-EXIT-REPORT.md`). The
writer round-trips the full modeled surface (semantic fixed point over 7/7 real
corpus documents, LibreOffice-valid). Long-tail semantic modeling continues
opportunistically with Retention as the no-silent-loss floor; the project pivots
to the rendering path (Phase 1C).

## Phase 1C–1E — Layout, pagination & rendering engine

**Design:** `43-PHASE-1C-LAYOUT-RENDERING-DESIGN.md` (research: `42-…`). A
production, Word-grade layout/pagination/rendering engine delivered in
production-complete slices; not an MVP. Retention remains the no-silent-loss
floor for unmodeled long-tail constructs.

| ID | Item | Owner | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1C-000 | Layout crate scaffold + type spine | Claude Code | Done | New `casual-doc-layout`: device-independent geometry (`Twip`/`Point`/`Size`/`Rect`), line-level types (`Glyph`/`GlyphRun`/`Line`/`LineLayout`) + the `LineShaper` seam, block/flow fragments (`BlockFragment`/`BoxMetrics`), immutable paginated output (`Page`/`PaginatedLayout`), the backend-neutral display list (`PaintItem`/`DisplayList`), and layout-side model anchors. Stack validated (parley 0.11 + tiny-skia 0.12 build + licenses in allow-list). 7 tests. |
| P1C-001 | `parley` line shaper (default `LineShaper`) | Claude Code | Done | Shape a styled paragraph → positioned lines (greedy UAX#14 break, bidi); behind the trait. |
| P1C-002 | Font faces — bold/italic rendering | Claude Code | Done | Bundled Roboto regular/bold/italic/bold-italic; shaper registers all 4 and selects per run from pushed weight/style; the resolved FontId rides the brush so `casual-doc-render` (BundledFontSource) outlines the SAME face. flow maps bold/italic -> face_id. Verified: Regular/Bold/Italic/BoldItalic render in the correct face. DOCX font-name matching (Calibri->Carlito etc.) + fallback = P1C-002b. |
| P1C-002b | Font resolution + fallback (DOCX font-name matching) | Claude Code | Done | New `resolve::FontResolver` (pure, WASM-safe, no system/fontconfig discovery): a run's declared family (direct `w:rFonts` or theme, resolved via `font_scheme`) → a concrete bundled face + disposition (exact/metric-compatible/fallback). Substitution table: Cambria→Caladea (metric-compatible, **Apache-2.0 Caladea now bundled**, `FontId 4..=7`); Calibri/Arial/Helvetica/Times New Roman/Courier → documented Roboto visual fallback. Per-glyph coverage fallback via `skrifa` cmap (already in-tree via parley): a codepoint missing from the resolved face (e.g. Cyrillic in Caladea) walks the family chain to a covering face, preserving bold/italic. `flow.rs` resolves each run and `shape.rs` pushes the resolved family so parley shapes the same face the renderer outlines. Deviations surfaced in a bounded, deterministic `FontResolutionReport` (`build_galley_with_report`). **Carlito NOT bundled: every published Carlito (Google Fonts, croscore, LibreOffice) is SIL-OFL-1.1, outside the deny.toml allowlist and the "no OFL/GPL fonts" rule — the design's "Carlito is Apache-2.0" premise is factually incorrect; Calibri stays on the visual fallback until an allowlisted Carlito is dropped in.** All gates green (fmt/clippy/test/doc/wasm/deny). 15 new tests. |
| P1C-002c | Bundle Carlito → metric-compatible Calibri | Claude Code | Done | Supersedes the P1C-002b Calibri caveat: OFL-1.1 is now cleared by the project owner (permissive, font-file-only, zero copyleft; fonts embed as `include_bytes!` asset bytes, not a crate, so `cargo-deny` never scans them — no `deny.toml` change). Bundled Carlito regular/bold/italic/bold-italic (`FontId 8..=11`) from `googlefonts/carlito` (`main` @ `3a810cab`, `fonts/ttf/`); OFL verified in each face's `name` table (ID 13/14 = SIL OFL 1.1 / scripts.sil.org/OFL). Resolver maps **Calibri → Carlito (metric-compatible)**, replacing the documented Roboto fallback; Cambria→Caladea unchanged; unknown families still fall back to Roboto and are reported. Carlito auto-registers in the shaper via `fonts::FAMILIES` (no `shape.rs` change). OFL text shipped at `fonts/LICENSES/OFL-1.1-Carlito.txt`; README updated. All gates green (fmt/clippy/test/doc/wasm/deny). |
| P1C-003 | Run/paragraph properties in layout + `TabResolver` | Claude Code | Done | Core run/paragraph styling, indentation/spacing, ordinary `w:tab` stops, leaders, and alignment are implemented. The distinct modeled `w:ptab` path remains open as `P1F-PTAB`. |
| P1C-RENDER | CPU render backend + first pixels | Claude Code | Done | New `casual-doc-render`: executes the display list on a `tiny-skia` pixmap; glyph runs rasterized from real `skrifa` outlines of the same face the shaper used (`GlyphSource` seam). `compose_paragraph` (layout) turns a shaped `LineLayout` into a display list. Pure walk of the immutable pages (no layout cost); everything in twips. Additive (new module + `mod hittest;`; `Glyph.cluster` doc clarified to node-relative). 9 tests. |
| P1E-002b | Byte-accurate shaper metadata (cluster/bidi/line-range) | Claude Code | Done | `ParleyShaper::shape_paragraph` now emits the real hit-testing metadata the `hittest.rs` contract consumes, replacing the three placeholders (`Glyph.cluster=0`, `GlyphRun.bidi_level=0`, whole-paragraph `Line.range`). Each glyph's `cluster` is a node-relative UTF-8 byte offset, mapped by walking the run's `parley` clusters in visual order (`Run::visual_clusters`) and tagging every glyph with its cluster's `text_range().start`; because the shaper concatenates a paragraph's runs in document order, a concatenated-text byte offset already equals the paragraph-node-relative caret anchor (`range.start.offset + byte`), so no per-run `StyledRun` remapping is needed and every existing call site is untouched. `GlyphRun.bidi_level` is the run's resolved direction parity (`Run::is_rtl()` → odd/even; parley exposes only parity publicly, which is all `hittest` reads). `Line.range` is the node-relative span of the line's `parley` `text_range()` (contiguous, non-overlapping, tiling the paragraph). 4 new tests: two-font line yields distinct increasing clusters; RTL/LTR/mixed bidi parity; contiguous sub-range lines; and an end-to-end `ParleyShaper`→`LayoutSnapshot` caret round-trip that lands on the correct byte. All gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy, test, doc, wasm). |

Verified: real-producer-rich.docx renders its table AND nested table with borders. Cross-page row split + header repeat = P1D-003. 1 test. |

| P1D-002b | Line-level paragraph splitting + widow/orphan | Claude Code | Done | Paginator rewritten as a state machine that splits a paragraph at line boundaries across pages (re-origining the carried lines), honoring keepLines (moved whole) and keepNext (kept together); widow/orphan control keeps >= 2 lines each side of a break; oversized content overflows (terminates). Golden invariant (incremental == full) is P1D-004. 9 tests. |
| P1F-6b | List numbering + bullet **rendering** (numId/ilvl markers) | Claude Code | Done | **Missing feature:** the model/import/writer carried numbering (P1A-014/P1B-005b/P1F-6) but `flow.rs` produced **no marker** — numbered headings (`1.`, `1.1`), bullet lists, and multi-level lists (`4.1`, `a)`, `i.`) rendered with nothing in every document (LibreOffice showed them; we didn't). New `numbering.rs` engine: `NumberingState` tracks per-`(numId, ilvl)` counters advanced once per numbered paragraph in document order (threaded through `FlowCtx`, so tables/SDTs that recurse the flow count too; intrinsic-width measurement uses a throwaway state; numbered paragraphs bypass the galley cache so a shifted sequence is never stale). Resolves `numId → NumberingInstance → AbstractNumbering → NumberingLevel` (with `lvlOverride`/`startOverride`), formats each active level via `numFmt` (**decimal, decimalZero, lowerLetter/upperLetter [bijective base-26], lowerRoman/upperRoman, ordinal, bullet, none**; cardinalText/ordinalText/unknown → decimal fallback) substituted into `lvlText` (`%1.%2` composition; `isLgl` forces all-decimal), and emits the marker as a glyph run built through the flow engine's font cascade + resolver (the level's `w:rPr` faces bullets in Symbol/Wingdings). The marker sits at the paragraph's first-line (hanging) indent; the level's `pPr` indent merges **below** the paragraph's own; the body's first-line indent is overridden to the hanging stop (`suff`=tab default, or after the marker for space/nothing) so the **body still soft-wraps** and continuation lines align. One localized hook in `flow_blocks`/`build_galley_cached` (`prepare_list_marker` + a `PreparedMarker::inject` into the first shaped line); all logic in `numbering.rs`. Verified vs the LibreOffice oracle (Sample Document 4.1–4.3 bullets •○▪– + decimal/lowerLetter/roman/upperLetter + restarted decimal; demo bulleted/numbered/multi-level/continued lists incl. wrapping + list continuation across an interrupting paragraph; SDS section headings `1.`/`2.`). No stray markers on non-list paragraphs; Class notes/Medical unaffected. 13 tests (11 numbering: formatting per numFmt, multi-level reset, independent-instance restart, isLgl, bullet-verbatim, body-indent geometry, level-indent surfacing; 2 flow: marker uses the level's rPr size + a plain paragraph gains none). **Deferrals:** `w:lvlRestart` (not modeled); cardinalText/ordinalText word-spelling; Symbol/Wingdings bullet glyphs render as tofu until the symbol font is provisioned (font-coverage, not numbering); long list items on a genuinely tab-stopped *body* still single-line (marker path wraps). |

| P1C-CJK | Normalize CJK fallback line metrics toward the run font (Word parity) | Claude Code | Done | **Line-metric fidelity:** a CJK run whose requested face is absent shapes with an OS/host *coverage-fallback* face (macOS PingFang/Arial-Unicode; browser Noto), whose native ascent+descent does not represent the run's nominal size — so mixed CJK+Latin lines inherited the tall bundled Roboto box (264 twips / ~17.6 px at 10 pt) and pure-CJK lines the over-tight fallback (206 / ~14 px), against LibreOffice's uniform ~16 px. `shape.rs` now shapes twice: pass 1 is byte-identical to before and detects whether the paragraph carries a CJK run on a **dynamic (non-bundled)** face (`is_cjk_scalar` ∧ non-bundled blob); only then does pass 2 re-shape with `LineHeight::FontSizeRelative(CJK_SINGLE_LINE_FACTOR=1.2 × w:spacing percent)`, so `parley` drives every line box from the *run font size* (Word's single line) and keeps the glyph baselines and box consistent on both the fast and flow-restacked paths. Gated so it never touches the bundled/deterministic path (CJK there is `.notdef` on a bundled face → no re-shape) or Latin runs (no CJK scalars): the 4 Latin corpus docs (Class notes, Sample, demo, Medical) render **pixel-identical** before/after. SDS CJK line pitch converges to ~16 px (LibreOffice parity); SDS single-column galley height −6 %. Page count (23) is still gated by multi-column section flow (separate work), as expected. 2 tests (bundled path unchanged; system-fonts CJK line == run-font single line, Latin not normalized); all gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy ±system-fonts, test, doc, wasm). |

## Phase 1F — Coverage Gaps (audit 2026-07-26)

Full evidence-backed register (39 rows, `file:line` cited) is `44-COVERAGE-GAP-AUDIT.md`;
this is the summary. Three structural findings drive the ordering: **F1** model-rich/
layout-poor (rendering data present but unconsumed — low-risk), **F2** silent data loss on
the semantic edit→save path (unmodeled parts dropped with no report), **F3** two active
correctness bugs (OMML math mangling; hidden `w:vanish` text rendered).

| ID | Title | Owner | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P1F-C1 | Math (OMML) namespace guard + opaque node | Claude Code | Done | **Correctness:** the body importer matched local element names only, so a math run's `m:r`/`m:t` were mistaken for `w:r`/`w:t` and flattened into garbled paragraph text. The read loop now detects an `m:oMath`/`m:oMathPara` root (`is_math_root`, gated by `math_allowed`) and buffers the entire subtree verbatim — the namespace guard: while capturing, no inner `m:` element reaches the `w:` arms. Modeled as a new opaque `InlineNode::Math` (`model/v1`, bounded by `MAX_MATH_BYTES`) retaining the OMML string plus a plain-text `m:t` fallback; the semantic exporter writes the OMML back with `xmlns:m` bound on the `w:document` root. Equation round-trips verbatim and its text never leaks. 2 tests (import mangling guard + semantic round trip). |
| P1F-C2 | Suppress hidden `w:vanish` text | Claude Code | Done | **Correctness:** `flow.rs push_styled_runs` (the shared run→shaped-text funnel) now skips a run only after resolving its effective properties through doc defaults, paragraph style, character style, and direct formatting. Inherited `w:vanish` therefore suppresses shaping/painting, while a direct `hidden=false` correctly restores visibility. 3 tests (collection unit, direct end-to-end glyph count, inherited/default override regression). |
| P1F-C3 | Implement `PushClip`/`PopClip` in renderer | Claude Code | Done | **Correctness:** the renderer's `PushClip`/`PopClip` were no-ops, so an `exact`-height row's clip was ignored at paint. Implemented a tiny-skia clip-mask stack (`build_clip_mask`, intersecting nested clips), threaded through every `Rect`/glyph paint call. Content beyond an `exact`-height clip rect is no longer drawn. 2 tests (clip prevents outside paint; popped clip unrestricts). `PaintItem::Image` remains a Tier-2 no-op. |
| P1F-C4 | Bound raster-image decoding | Codex | Done | **Security/resource correctness:** the CPU renderer previously called `image::load_from_memory`, relying only on the dependency's 512 MiB default allocation cap and imposing no project dimension/pixel ceiling; a small encoded PNG/JPEG with huge dimensions could therefore consume hundreds of MiB per paint. `decode_to_pixmap` now reads dimensions before full decode, enforces the documented secure defaults (32,768 px per axis and 100 megapixels), and configures the decoder with the corresponding 400,000,000-byte allocation ceiling. Over-budget media uses the existing graceful-skip behavior. 2 tests cover an actual over-wide PNG regression plus accepted/rejected dimension and pixel boundaries. |

| P1F-11 | Inline images decoded + blitted in render | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1):** every embedded logo/figure was blank — the `PaintItem::Image` arm was a no-op, `flow.rs` ignored `InlineNode::Drawing`, and no image paint item was ever constructed. Now: `flow.rs collect_items` maps an inline `w:drawing` to a `FlowItem::Image` (media id resolved to its package part name via `Definitions.media`; `wp:extent` EMU → twips at 635 EMU/twip), laid out as an inline-box line (`text.rs InlineImage` on `Line.images`, stacked in `shape_paragraph_items`) and composed to a `PaintItem::Image` carrying the extent-derived rect (`compose.rs`); the `paragraph_hash` cache key folds the media+size. The renderer gains a `MediaSource` seam (image analogue of `GlyphSource`; `NoMediaSource`/`MapMediaSource` impls) — `render` decodes PNG/JPEG bytes to premultiplied RGBA (the `image` crate, `default-features=false`, `png`+`jpeg` only — MIT/Apache, WASM-clean, `cargo deny` allowlisted) and blits them scaled into the box via tiny-skia `draw_pixmap`, honoring the clip stack; unknown/undecodable media (incl. deferred EMF/WMF vector metafiles, **P1F-28**) renders nothing without panicking. The `render_docx_page` example serves `word/media/*` from the package. Anchored/floating drawings stay out of scope (P1F-28). 5 tests (PNG blit ink-in-rect/bg-out, JPEG likewise, unknown+undecodable graceful, inline drawing → image paint item carries the extent-derived rect); wasm32 + deny green with the new decoder. **Follow-up (this PR, `agent/fix-emf-wmf-placeholder`):** "unknown/undecodable renders nothing" silently conflated two different cases. `render_image` now distinguishes them: no media bytes at all still renders nothing (there is genuinely nothing to show); bytes present but not decodable by the PNG/JPEG-only path (EMF/WMF, GIF, BMP, TIFF, a corrupt file, or one rejected by the dimension/pixel-budget guard) now paints a visible, deterministic placeholder — a stroked border box with a corner-to-corner diagonal cross, in the same never-silently-drop spirit as `casual-doc-layout::symbol_map`'s `.notdef` `□` glyph — filling the image's known rect instead of leaving a blank gap. This is a rendering-only fix: real EMF/WMF vector-metafile decoding is still not implemented (still P1F-28/future work), and the fix does not reach the import-time `CompatibilityReport` (`casual-doc-import/src/report.rs`) — that mechanism is entirely scoped to import and isn't threaded through `casual-doc-layout`/`render`'s `DisplayList`/`MediaSource` seam, so wiring render-time disposition into it is a larger plumbing change, deferred and noted as a known gap. 6 tests total for the image path (split the old combined "unknown or undecodable" test into "unknown media still renders nothing" + "undecodable-but-present renders a placeholder"; updated the dimension-limit-rejection test to expect a placeholder instead of blank, since a rejected-for-safety decode is also "bytes present, can't decode"). |
| P1F-IMG-DECODE-GBT | Decode GIF/BMP/TIFF inline images | Claude Code | Done | Closes the GIF/BMP/TIFF leg of the docs/55 §8 image-decode residual. P1F-11 enabled only the `png`/`jpeg` codec features, so GIF/BMP/TIFF pictures — common in Office-authored DOCX (pasted clip-art, scanned pages) — fell through the generic `decode_to_pixmap` to the P1F-11 placeholder box. `decode_to_pixmap` is fully format-generic (`image::guess_format` + `ImageReader::with_format().decode()` under the existing dimension/pixel/alloc budget guards, which are codec-agnostic), so the fix is enabling the `gif`+`bmp`+`tiff` `image`-crate features in `casual-doc-render/Cargo.toml`; no decode-logic change. The added codecs are pure-Rust and WASM-clean (`wasm32-unknown-unknown` builds), MIT/Apache and `cargo deny check licenses bans` green. Genuinely undecodable/unsupported formats (EMF/WMF vector metafiles, SVG) still paint the P1F-11 placeholder — real metafile/SVG decode remains future work (SVG additionally gated on an MPL-2.0 allowlist decision). Verified: a new render test decodes+blits a solid GIF/BMP/TIFF raster (red ink inside the box, white outside), `cargo +1.96.0 fmt --check`, strict workspace clippy, `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` (0 failures), `cargo deny`, wasm32 build. |
| P1F-12 | Paragraph indents applied in layout/paint | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1):** `flow.rs line_constraints` now reduces the shaper wrap width by start+end indent and maps `w:ind@firstLine`/`@hanging` to a signed first-line indent (hanging wins); the `ParleyShaper` applies it via `Layout::set_text_indent` (correct per-line wrap width) and `compose.rs` shifts the paragraph origin by the start indent. 4 tests: shaper first-line-right, hanging-left; flow start/end indent narrows the column + carries into box metrics; compose start-indent offsets the run. |
| P1F-13 | Tab stops resolved in layout (custom/default/leaders/alignment) | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F2):** `flow.rs collect_runs` mapped `w:tab` to a literal `"\t"` (parley treated it as whitespace) and `ParagraphProperties.tabs`/`w:defaultTabStop` were unread, so tabbed content collapsed. A new caller-side layer (`tabs.rs`, layered over the base `LineShaper` per its trait contract) resolves tabs to real advances: `collect_items` flattens inlines to a `FlowItem` stream (runs + tabs + breaks), and `shape_with_flow` splits it into segments at each tab, measures each segment unwrapped with the base shaper, and positions the following segment by the resolved stop's alignment — **left/center/right/decimal** (decimal aligns the `.` glyph's x) — honoring explicit `w:tabs` and, past the last explicit stop, the document `w:defaultTabStop` (720-twip fallback). **Leaders** (dot/hyphen/underscore/middleDot) are tiled as glyph runs filling the gap; **bar** stops are carried per-line (`Line.bars`) and drawn by `compose.rs` as vertical rules. Ordinary text (no tab/break/bar) keeps the fast path — byte-identical output. The `GalleyCache` key (`paragraph_hash`) folds in the item stream, tab stops, and default interval. The current follow-up translates margin-relative ordinary stops into hanging-indent coordinates and soft-wraps an overflowing trailing value at its value column; general RTL tab layout remains open. |
| P1F-14 | Hard breaks (`w:br` line/page/column, `w:cr`) in layout + paginator | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F2):** `flow.rs` ignored `InlineNode::Break` (text merged) and `LineBreak::Hard` was never emitted. `shape_with_flow` (see P1F-13) splits the paragraph at each break into forced lines: `w:br` textWrapping and `w:cr` (both `BreakKind::Line`) emit `LineBreak::Hard`; `w:br` type **page**/**column** additionally set a new `Line.page_break_after` marker (columns collapse to pages while single-column). The marker threads through the galley to `paginate.rs place_paragraph`, which caps the greedy line-take at the break line and flushes — a forced split integrated into the **existing** line-split machinery, so the incremental `repaginate == paginate` golden is unchanged (verified). Routing to the line splitter also triggers on a forced break even under `keepLines`/keep-groups so the explicit break wins. 6 tests (line-break → two lines, page-break marker, flow integration for line/page, paginate mid-paragraph split, incremental golden with a forced-break paragraph). |

| P1F-17 | Headers/footers laid out + painted; PAGE/NUMPAGES fields resolved | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1) + fields.** `Page` gained `header`/`footer` bands and `PageConfig` reserves fixed per-section header/footer band heights (`content_area` shrinks by both; `header_band`/`footer_band` rects). Header/footer content flows through the **identical** shared pipeline as the body — `flow::flow_header_footer` → `flow_blocks`/`flow_table` recursion — so a header/footer is a normal `Vec<BlockFragment>` supporting paragraphs, **tables (incl. nested)**, **inline images** (P1F-11), borders/shading, and every inline construct; no reduced header path (future body constructs like text boxes work automatically). Per-page **selection** (default vs `titlePg` first-page vs `w:evenAndOddHeaders` even/odd) and placement are a **post-pagination** pass (`running::place_running_content`) keyed on each page's *final* number — never baked during pagination — so a reused/renumbered page always shows the right variant. **PAGE/NUMPAGES**: `InlineNode::Field` now flows to an inline field run carrying only a *marker* (kind + style + placeholder), laid out by a fielded-paragraph path (runs + tabs + fields on one line); `paginate::resolve_fields` is a **pure, idempotent** post-pass that stamps each PAGE with the page number and NUMPAGES with the total and reshapes the glyph run in place (trailing runs reflow contiguously from a stored anchor). Because both passes are pure functions of the final page list, they apply identically after `paginate` and `repaginate` — the incremental `repaginate == paginate` golden **still holds with headers/footers + resolved fields**, and an edit that changes the page count updates NUMPAGES on **every** page incl. reused ones (`compose_page` paints header → body → footer). Pagination halt/prefix/splice logic is untouched (only band reservation + the two post-passes added). 12 tests (3-page header/footer on every page + body shrink, `titlePg`, even/odd, `Page X of Y` per-page + NUMPAGES==total, multi-digit reflow, body PAGE field, header image, header nested table, field-pass idempotence, incremental golden across a page-count-changing edit). Single-section; multi-section running content and soft-wrapped fielded paragraphs are follow-ups. |
| P1F-18 | Super/subscript + `w:position`, all-caps / small-caps consumed in layout | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1):** the run-appearance properties were modeled but the flow layer ignored them, so super/subscripts sat on the baseline at full size, `w:position` did nothing, and `w:caps`/`w:smallCaps` runs rendered lowercase. Now `flow.rs styled_run` folds `w:vertAlign` into a reduced shaped size (~2/3) plus a `baseline_shift` (raise ~1/3 for super, lower ~1/6 for sub) and adds the `w:position` half-point raise/lower with **no** resize; `StyledRun` carries a new `baseline_shift` (twips, +up) that `shape.rs` applies at the glyph-run origin (screen-y down, so it subtracts) via the shaper brush. `StyledRun.text` became a `Cow<str>` so case transforms own their rewritten text while untransformed runs stay borrowed (zero-cost): `w:caps` uppercases the run; `w:smallCaps` uppercases **and** splits the run into originally-lowercase spans (shaped at ~3/4 size) vs the rest (full size). The `GalleyCache` key folds `baseline_shift`. 5 tests (superscript raise+shrink, subscript lower, `w:position` raise without resize, all-caps glyph-equality vs literal uppercase, small-caps 3/4 split sizing). Line-metric growth from a tall raise and per-cluster offset drift under non-ASCII case folding are follow-ups. |
| P1F-23 | Theme / auto run color resolved against the real `clrScheme` palette | Claude Code | Done — wired | **Paint fidelity (F1):** the `a:clrScheme` palette was modeled (P1F-3) but layout never consumed it, so every `w:themeColor` run resolved to black. `build_galley` (and the `_with_report`/cached/header-footer variants) now resolves `Definitions.color_scheme` **once** into a `ResolvedPalette` (`[u8;4]` per slot — `srgbClr` → its RGB, `sysClr` → its `lastClr` or a conventional default) and threads `Option<&ResolvedPalette>` through `FlowCtx`, passing the **real** palette. `styled_run` resolves a run color across every model variant: `Color::Theme(slot)` → the slot's resolved RGB (routed through an `apply_tint_shade` seam, ready for when the model carries theme tint/shade), `Color::Rgb` → itself, unset/`auto`/no-palette → black — no silent black for a resolvable theme color. 4 tests (**end-to-end**: a document whose `clrScheme` accent-1 is a distinct color, referenced by a themed run, flows via `build_galley` to that exact RGB, not black; sysClr→lastClr; tint-lightens/shade-darkens; auto/unresolvable→black). |
| P1F-24 | System-font fallback tier (CJK / complex-script / symbol coverage) | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1):** the shaper/renderer served only the bundled Latin faces (Roboto/Caladea/Carlito), so any run of CJK / symbol / complex-script text rendered as `.notdef` tofu (▯) — layout correct, glyph coverage missing. Now a **general dynamic font registry** (`casual_doc_layout::font_registry::FontRegistry`) is the one seam by which the shaper and the renderer agree on the bytes behind every face, populated three ways: **bundled** (fixed `FontId 0..=11`, served from `fonts::face_bytes`), **system** (native, feature-gated), and **host-registered** (runtime blobs). New feature-gated `system-fonts` (`casual-doc-layout`/`casual-doc-render`, `= ["parley/system"]`) turns on `fontique`'s OS font source as a fallback *after* the bundled faces; `parley` shapes a run's uncovered code points with a covering OS font. The shaper reads the face `parley` **actually** resolved (`run.font()` — a bundled blob keeps its bundled `FontId`, so the golden/bundled path is byte-for-byte unchanged; any other face — an OS fallback or a host blob — is **interned** into the registry under a dynamic `FontId ≥ 0x1000_0000`, disjoint from the bundled block) and records `.notdef` code points as a **coverage gap** (`registry.missing_coverage()`) a host queries to know what to fetch. `casual-doc-render` gains `RegistryFontSource` (serves bundled bytes for bundled ids, snapshotted dynamic bytes+face-index for dynamic ids) and a `GlyphSource::face_index` seam so `skrifa::FontRef::from_index` selects the right face inside a `.ttc` collection (OS CJK fonts). Host path (`ParleyShaper::register_font` / `register_fallback_font`) registers a runtime blob into the collection (and, for fallback, wires it for the given ISO-15924 scripts via `append_fallbacks`) and interns it — the **same seam** the later browser/WASM slice reuses to feed network-fetched Noto CJK, zero rework. **Determinism/WASM intact:** default OFF → bundled-only, deterministic; the system source is fully compiled out under `wasm32` and feature-off (both `cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` and `--all-features` are clean); the registry/host path is a platform-free in-memory blob store that compiles on wasm. `render_docx_page` example serves fonts through the registry and enables the feature (`cargo run --example render_docx_page --features system-fonts` renders CJK). Tests: (feature ON, native) `中文` shapes to non-`.notdef` glyphs via a dynamically interned OS face and rasterizes real ink, not tofu; (feature OFF, deterministic) `中文` → `.notdef` + coverage gap recorded, bundled `FontId` kept; host-registered blob interned + served + rasterized through `RegistryFontSource`; registry intern/dedup/snapshot/coverage units. All gates green in **both** feature states (fmt 1.96.0, clippy, workspace test, doc, wasm ×2, deny — the system source's platform crates were already license-allowlisted). |
| P1F-25 | Table cell margins (`w:tcMar`/`w:tblCellMar`) + vertical alignment (`w:vAlign`) applied in layout | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1, docs/46 §F3):** the model already carried per-cell `w:tcMar`, table-default `w:tblCellMar`, and `w:vAlign`, but layout dropped all three — cell text hugged the top-left grid line ("cramped table" look) and vertically-aligned labels never sat on their answer line. Now `flow.rs flow_table` resolves each cell's effective margins by precedence (cell `w:tcMar` ⊕ table `w:tblCellMar` ⊕ Word default L/R 108, T/B 0; `resolve_cell_margins`), flows the cell content through the **same** shared pipeline at the reduced inner width (`cell_width − start − end`), and records the margins + a `CellVAlign` (`cell_vertical_alignment`) on `CellFragment` (new additive fields, strict-serde, skip-when-default so existing goldens are unchanged). `block.rs cells_content_height` now counts each cell's top+bottom margins so `resolve_row_height` measures rows correctly against `w:trHeight` (atLeast/exact logic untouched). Composition (`compose.rs`) offsets the flowed content by the start margin (x) and, once the row height is known, by `CellFragment::content_y_offset` = top margin + the `w:vAlign` share of the leftover slack (top→0, center→½, bottom→all); shading/border/clip rects still span the whole cell. Column widths, `w:gridSpan` distribution, and border-conflict resolution are unchanged. Medical-incident-form probe: cell text no longer hugs the grid line, header bands are inset + vertically centered, bottom-aligned labels sit on the answer line (page count 3, LibreOffice oracle 4 — margins add height without forcing the split). 2 focused unit tests (margin-precedence resolution; vAlign slack placement top/center/bottom + occupied-height). All gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy -D warnings, workspace test, doc, wasm check). |

Now: a new additive `InlineNode::EmbeddedObject { kind, part, extra_parts, preview, extent, prog_id }` (model v1, strict serde, validated — part pointers bounded like `MediaReference`, extent in `0..=MAX_EMU`, preview resolves in `Definitions.media`). Import (`body.rs`) resolves the `c:chart`/`dgm:relIds` `r:id`(s) to part names via a new main-document `embedded_index` and builds the node instead of reporting; the body parser records the referenced part names so `build_retained_parts` **un-orphans** exactly those (kept in the preserved-bytes set, excluded from the orphan-rel set — coordinated with the side-table so each part's relationship is emitted **once**, from the node). Export (`semantic.rs`) writes the `w:drawing`/`wp:inline`/`a:graphicData` frame with the `c:chart`/`dgm:relIds` reference and emits the referencing relationship(s) in `document.xml.rels` with the verbatim `r:id`; the part bytes come from the side-table. Round-trips as an editable reference (node survives, part preserved AND referenced, package reopens). Anchored/floating positioning stays P1F-28. Tests: chart + diagram import→write→reopen (kind, extent, referenced parts, no double-rel, no drop report). |

Import reads the `o:OLEObject` `r:id`+`ProgID`, the `v:imagedata` preview (through the media table), and the origin size; export re-emits the `w:object` with the preview shape and `o:OLEObject`. Round-trips with progId + preview referenced, embedding un-orphaned. Test: OLE import→write→reopen. |
| P1F-28 | Anchored/floating drawing positioning (first cut) | Claude Code | Done | **Image positioning (F1) + data loss (F2):** an anchored `w:drawing` (`wp:anchor`) collapsed to inline — its `wp:positionH`/`wp:positionV`/`wp:wrap*`/`@behindDoc`/`wp:docPr@descr` hit `is_drawing_scaffolding` and were swallowed, so a floating logo/figure rendered inline (wrong place) and its alt text vanished. Now an anchored picture is a first-class node. **Model** (v1, additive, strict serde, validated): `InlineNode::AnchoredDrawing { id, media, extent, anchor, descr }` with `DrawingAnchor { horizontal, vertical, wrap, behind_doc }` — `AnchorHorizontal`/`AnchorVertical` = a `relativeFrom` (page/margin/column/character/L·R·inside·outsideMargin; page/margin/paragraph/line/T·B·inside·outsideMargin) + a `HorizontalPosition`/`VerticalPosition` (`Offset(i64 EMU, signed)` | `Align`), a `WrapMode` enum (square/tight/through/topAndBottom/none — modeled for fidelity even though wrap-around isn't laid out yet), and `behind_doc` z-order; `descr` alt text (≤`MAX_DESCR_BYTES`, accessibility). Media resolves in `Definitions.media`, extent/offsets bounded to `±MAX_EMU`. Inline `Drawing` is unchanged. **Import** (`body.rs`): `wp:anchor` opens a `PendingAnchor` (vs `wp:inline` → the inline path); `positionH`/`positionV` capture `relativeFrom` + the `posOffset`/`align` element text; `wrap*` the mode; `@behindDoc` the z-order (explicit truthy, not toggle `is_true`); `docPr@descr` the alt text (on an anchor it's modeled, not reported); `commit_drawing` builds `Segment::AnchoredDrawing` for a resolvable blip. **Export** (`semantic.rs`): re-emits `w:drawing`/`wp:anchor` with positionH/V, extent, wrap, `@behindDoc`, `docPr@descr`, sharing the picture frame with the inline writer (`write_pic_graphic`). **Layout/render (first cut — position, not wrap):** a new post-pagination pass `anchor::place_anchored_drawings` walks the doc for anchors, finds the page each one's anchoring paragraph landed on, resolves an absolute page-local rect from `relativeFrom`+offset/align against the page/margin/paragraph box, and records a `PlacedAnchor` on `Page.anchored` (additive, off the pagination hot path so the incremental halt is untouched — `paginate`/`repaginate` produce empty `anchored`, filled identically after); `compose_page` paints behind-doc anchors before and in-front anchors after the text (z-order). Anchored images are removed from inline flow (they don't consume a line box); text does NOT yet re-flow around them (**wrap-around is the P1F-28 follow-up**), and multi-section per-anchor geometry is a follow-up. The `render_docx_page` example runs the pass. 12 tests: model (offset/align/z-order JSON round-trip, dangling media rejected, offset out-of-domain rejected); import (posOffset+relativeFrom+wrap+behindDoc+descr mapped, not reported; align+wrapSquare+default z-order); export (import→write→reopen semantic fixed point incl. negative offset); layout (anchor resolution page/margin/negative/center; composes a `PaintItem::Image` at the resolved rect not the inline cursor + not flowed inline; behindDoc paint-order vs text). |
| P1F-29 | Floating-table position (`w:tblpPr`) modeled as first-class placement | Claude Code | Done | **Silent data loss (F2):** a positioned (floating) table's `w:tblpPr` hit no `tblPr`-child arm, so on edit→save the table snapped back into inline block flow — its anchor, offset, and text-wrap distances vanished. `TableProperties` modeled `w:tblOverlap` but had no float position. Now `TableProperties` carries an additive `float_position: Option<TableFloatPosition>` (model v1, strict serde, `deny_unknown_fields`, validated). `TableFloatPosition` (`CT_TblPPr`, §17.4.58) holds `horz_anchor`/`vert_anchor` (`TableAnchor` = text/margin/page), the absolute signed-twip offsets `tbl_px_twips`/`tbl_py_twips` (`w:tblpX`/`w:tblpY`), the mutually-exclusive named alignments `x_spec` (`TableXAlign` = left/center/right/inside/outside) / `y_spec` (`TableYAlign` = inline/top/center/bottom/inside/outside) which win over the offsets, and the four `*_from_text_twips` wrap distances. Import (`body.rs`/`tables.rs`) parses every `w:tblpPr` attribute (unknown anchor/spec tokens drop that attribute to `None`; signed offsets clamp to `-31_680..=31_680`, from-text distances to `0..=31_680` — the twip bounds the sibling table props already enforce). Model/import/export only — layout unchanged. Tests: import mapping + clamp/unknown-token drop; a `w:tblpPr` (both anchors + absolute X + named Y + all four fromText) import→write→reopen semantic fixed point. |
| P1F-31 | Symbols (`w:sym`) modeled as first-class glyphs | Claude Code | Done | **Data loss (F2):** a `w:sym` (a font + a `w:char` code point — e.g. a Wingdings checkmark/bullet, usually a PUA `0xF0xx` glyph) hit the run catch-all in `body.rs` and vanished on edit→save, since the special glyph is representable only as a font-bound code point, never as ordinary run text. | P1F-31b | Symbols (`w:sym`) **rendering** via Unicode mapping | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1), the P1F-31 follow-up:** `InlineNode::Symbol` had **zero** handling in `flow.rs`, so every modeled `w:sym` glyph was silently dropped at layout — the Medical form's category checkboxes (`Wingdings 2` `F0A3`/`F052`) and Wingdings/Symbol bullets rendered as nothing; and (per P1F-6b's deferral) numbering bullets in a Symbol/Wingdings face rendered as tofu. Since the legacy symbol fonts are not bundled, the raw PUA code point cannot be drawn by a text face. New `casual-doc-layout::symbol_map`: comprehensive **font-gated** byte→Unicode tables for `Symbol` (Adobe authoritative — full Greek/math/arrows/card-suits), `Wingdings`, `Wingdings 2`, `Wingdings 3`, and `Webdings` (transcribed from the published dingbat→Unicode mapping), plus a `coverage_fallback` that folds the code points Unicode assigned in poorly-covered blocks (the Unicode-7 dingbats `U+1F5xx`/`U+1F7xx`, `U+2Bxx`, glyph-fragment PUA) down to the well-covered BMP glyph that reads the same (`U+1F5F9`→`☑`, `U+26AB`→`●`, triangle-headed arrows→`←↑→↓`, …) so a bundled/OS face actually draws them. `map_symbol(font, code)` returns `None` for an ordinary (non-symbol) font so literal bullets/numbers pass through untouched; `resolve_symbol` guarantees a **visible** glyph — the mapped Unicode, else a recovered printable-ASCII alias, else a `□` (U+25A1) placeholder — so a symbol is never silently dropped. `flow.rs`: `collect_runs`/`collect_items` emit a styled glyph run for `InlineNode::Symbol` (default run props; the mapped Unicode intentionally resolves against a covering **text** face, not the unbundled symbol font); `prepare_list_marker` routes a bullet marker's glyph through the same map (dropping the symbol face when remapped) and **down-scales** full-em geometric bullets (`●○■◆◊`) to ~0.62× so a bullet is a small dot/ring, matching Word/LibreOffice, not a full-height shape. Verified (render + LibreOffice oracle, `system-fonts`): Medical form category checklist gains a `☐` before all six items + the YES/`☐`NO line (was blank); Sample §12.1 native-checkbox lines gain `☐` (was blank); Sample §4.2 sub-list `○` scaled to a small ring; no change to non-symbol pages (Sample §5 tables identical vs the clean base). 12 `symbol_map` unit tests (Wingdings-2 `F0A3`/`F052`/`F053`; Symbol Greek/math/arrow/bullet/suit; Wingdings check/cross/circle; Wingdings-3 arrow→BMP; unmapped→visible placeholder not dropped; every table entry resolves to a non-PUA glyph) + 1 `flow` test (a Symbol node emits a glyph run; an untabulated code → placeholder). All gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy, workspace test, doc, wasm). **Deferrals:** the `Symbol` node carries no run `rPr` in schema v1, so a symbol glyph shapes at the document default size/color (surrounding-run size/color is a later model extension); the long tail of genuinely pictographic dingbats (weather/vehicles/faces) keeps its true — often astral — code point (renders on a capable system font, else a placeholder box). |

| P1F-BUG-01 | Fix inline-formatting overprint (dense runs / inverse-video drawn on top of each other) | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1) regression fix:** dense inline formatting rendered overprinted — adjacent runs sharing one face but differing only in brush/decoration (e.g. a `w:highlight` span beside a recolored "inverse video" white-on-black span) drew on top of each other, unreadable. Root cause in `shape.rs`: `parley` splits a single shaping `Run` into one `PositionedLayoutItem::GlyphRun` **per contiguous style span** (a brush/color/highlight/decoration change splits the run *without* re-shaping, preserving kerning), each `GlyphRun` covering only a `glyph_start`/`glyph_count` **slice** of the run's glyphs. The shaper built each output run from `glyph_run.run().visual_clusters()` — the **whole** parent run — so every brush span re-emitted *all* the run's glyphs at overlapping x, painting the merged text once per span. Fixed to emit only this `GlyphRun`'s slice via `glyph_run.glyphs()`, pairing each glyph with its cluster byte offset from a per-run offset stream consumed in lockstep (run boundary detected by `run().text_range()`; `GlyphRun`s of one run are emitted consecutively). Layout/render only — no model/import/export change; pagination halt logic untouched. 2 regression tests (`flow.rs`): dense same-face runs (red/strike/superscript/highlight/inverse) have pairwise-disjoint glyph cluster ranges and x-monotonic origins (the exact invariant the bug violated — verified failing before the fix); consecutive non-empty paragraphs keep positive distinct heights. Before/after confirmed by rendering the inverse-video paragraph through the full import→galley→paginate→compose→render pipeline (garbled self-overlap → clean left-to-right text). |
| P1F-39 | `w:altChunk` + non-breaking/soft hyphens (`w:noBreakHyphen`/`w:softHyphen`) + absolute-position tab (`w:ptab`) | Claude Code | Done | **Data loss (F2):** all three hit a catch-all and vanished. | P1F-BANDFIX | Header/footer bands nested inside page margins (Word geometry) | Claude Code | Done | **Systematic over-pagination (correctness).** `PageConfig::content_area` subtracted the measured header/footer band heights **on top of** the full top/bottom margins, so every header/footer page lost `header_height + footer_height` of body area and over-paginated. Word instead **nests** the bands inside the margins: `body_top = max(margin_top, header_distance + header_height)`, `body_bottom = max(margin_bottom, footer_distance + footer_height)`, where `header_distance`/`footer_distance` are the `w:pgMar/@w:header`/`@w:footer` values (default 720 twips). A band shorter than its margin — the common case — now costs the body nothing. Root cause was also in the model: `PageMargins` (`model/v1/definitions.rs`) lacked the header/footer distances, so layout couldn't apply the `max()`. Added additive schema-v1 `header_twips`/`footer_twips: Option<i32>` (strict serde, default `None`); import (`import/body.rs` pgMar) parses `w:header`/`w:footer`; writer (`export/semantic.rs`) re-emits them; `PageConfig` gained `header_distance`/`footer_distance`, and `header_band`/`footer_band` now anchor at those distances (header at `header_distance`, footer at `page_h − footer_distance − footer_height`), kept consistent with `content_area`. `document_layout.rs` supplies the distances from the parsed margins (720 default). Incremental halt/prefix/splice core untouched (geometry-only). Corpus (LibreOffice oracle): SDS_ANTI-T…_ZH 20→**16** pages (=oracle; residual CJK line-height is a separate fix), Medical-In…orm 3→3 (oracle 4, no regression), Sample Document 22→22 (oracle 26, no regression). Extended the pgMar import + semantic-round-trip tests to assert the distances survive; all workspace tests green (no golden needed changing — the header/footer reservation fixtures pin the band flush to the margin, which under the nesting rule still reserves the full band and keeps every assertion valid). |

A CSS-like `style` parser converts pt/in/cm/mm/px/pc/emu/twip → twips; `v:group` `coordorigin`/`coordsize` transforms are flattened so every child shape is emitted at absolute twips. Best-effort/infallible (a malformed fragment yields the parsed prefix). **VML kinds found in the SDS:** `v:rect` (15 body + 1 header + footer rects; solid-fill horizon rules and grey footer bar), `v:group` (3 body + 1 footer; callout-box and rule coordinate frames), `v:shape` (4 body + 4 header + 1 footer; `path`-outline callouts and `type="#_x0000_t202"` text boxes), `v:shapetype` (the `_x0000_t202` text-box template, skipped so its stroke/path don't leak), `v:textbox`/`w:txbxContent` (header/footer/callout text), `v:fill`/`v:path`/`v:stroke`, `w10:wrap` (`topAndBottom`/`none`) — no `v:line`/`v:oval`/`v:roundrect`/`v:imagedata` occur in this doc (horizon rules are thin filled rects), but all are parsed for corpus coverage. 15 unit tests (real SDS rect/group/path-shape/text-box fragments + synthetic line/oval/roundrect/imagedata/opacity/unit-conversion/style-edge-case/shapetype-leak/malformed cases). **Follow-up (on top of F4):** wire `VmlDrawing` into the floating-object layer — rect/line/oval/shape → shape paint; `image_rid` → positioned image via the shared media index; `textbox` → flow `content_xml` through the shared block pipeline at the VML box; `z_index<0` → `behindDoc`. Module-level doc-comment carries the full integration spec. No layout/paint/`compose`/`anchor`/model changes in the parse slice. **Paint integration (this slice, `feat/vml-paint`):** `commit_pict` in `body.rs` now re-parses each closed `w:pict`'s raw XML (mirrored event-by-event into a `Writer` by `capture_pict_event`, teed in the `run` loop alongside normal dispatch so the inline `v:imagedata` path and the `w:txbxContent` block flow are unaffected) and maps every `VmlDrawing` onto the EXISTING F4 float model — REUSING `InlineNode::Group`/`AnchoredDrawing`/`TextBox`, `GroupShape`/`ShapeGeometry`/`Rgba`/`ShapeStroke`/`PointEmu`, no new model or paint types: a `v:rect`/`v:roundrect`/`v:oval` → a standalone anchored `GroupShape` (a group-of-one, identity transform) with `ShapeGeometry::Rectangle`/`RoundRectangle`/`Ellipse` + resolved `Rgba` fill/`ShapeStroke`; `v:line` → a `Line` shape float sized to the segment's bounding box; `v:imagedata@r:id` → a positioned `AnchoredDrawing` resolved through the shared media index (a genuinely inline image with no VML box keeps the legacy inline `Drawing`); `v:textbox` → a floating `TextBox` whose `w:txbxContent` blocks are redirected by `exit_frame` (when inside an open pict) and re-attached at the VML box, flowed through the SAME shared block pipeline (uniform-flow invariant). `VmlPosition` twips → EMU offsets; `h_relative`/`v_relative` → the F4 anchor frames (page/margin/column/text/paragraph/line, text/column→margin fallback); `z_index<0` → `behind_doc`, the signed `z-index` → the monotonic `relativeHeight` z-key order-preservingly. Header/footer VML flows through F4's `place_floats` band walk unchanged (the SDS header rules + date/title text boxes now paint). **Deferrals:** a generic `v:shape` `path` is approximated by the OUTLINE (hairline stroke) of its bounding box rather than a solid fill — this reproduces the SDS callout-box frame LibreOffice draws without blackening the box; honoring the exact path, gradient fills, and anti-diagonal `v:line` orientation are follow-ups. **Verify (SDS page 0 vs LibreOffice):** horizon rules, the callout-box outline, the right-positioned 修订/打印日期 header text boxes, and the grey footer bar now paint where LO shows them; SDS stays 16 pages (oracle 16); Medical 3 / demo 8 / Sample 26 / Class notes 1 unchanged. 3 new import tests (`v:rect` horizon rule → behind-text shape float; `v:imagedata` → positioned picture; `v:textbox` → floating text box with flowed content) + the 2 pre-existing inline-VML-image tests preserved. **Regression fix (`fix/vml-textbox-overlap`):** floating `v:textbox` placement was reverted — on real VML-primary docs (the Chinese SDS) the absolute VML box positions overlap each other AND the body text, turning content pages 2–16 into overprinted mush. | P1F-PAGINATION | Content-per-page correctness: section breaks, empty-para height, Arial resolution, CJK line overlap | Claude Code | Done | **Pagination correctness (content lands on the right page, not just matching page COUNT).** Owner report: page counts matched the LibreOffice oracle while content-per-page was wrong (e.g. `Sample Document.docx` crammed the cover AND the Table of Contents onto page 1). Root causes + fixes, verified by rendering each page vs LibreOffice PDF pages (`--features casual-doc-layout/system-fonts`, `soffice --convert-to pdf` + `pdftoppm -r 96`): **(1) Paragraph-embedded section breaks were ignored.** The cover→TOC boundary is a `w:sectPr` in a paragraph's `w:pPr` (a `nextPage` section break), not a `w:br`. `paginate_document` builds one galley + one page config and never consulted `properties.section_break`, so the break was dropped. Fix: `flow.rs` `apply_section_break` stamps `page_break_after` on the last line of a paragraph that ends a section whose **successor** section's `w:type` starts a new page (`nextPage`/`evenPage`/`oddPage`/unspecified; `continuous`/`nextColumn` do not), reading `document.definitions().sections` (threaded onto `FlowCtx.sections`; empty for running content). Applied in both the fresh (`flow_blocks`) and incremental (`build_galley_cached`) paths; the incremental path keeps the cache entry section-break-pure and re-stamps on every build (a neighbouring paragraph's section-type edit can flip this paragraph's break without dirtying it), so it never serves a stale flag. **(2) The paginator dropped a forced break on a paragraph's *final* line.** `place()` only routed a paragraph through the line splitter when `lines.len() > 1`, so a single-line paragraph (an empty cover `sectPr` para, or a lone `w:br`) carrying `page_break_after` fell to the whole-place arm which ignored the flag; and even in `place_paragraph` a forced break on the last line never flushed. Fix (`paginate.rs`): route through the splitter whenever **any** line has `page_break_after` (any line count), and flush after a forced break that lands on the paragraph's final line (`start == n || forced`) — a flush with nothing left to place is a no-op, so a document-final break adds no blank page. **(3) A model-empty paragraph collapsed to parley's ~21-twip default.** `ParleyShaper::shape_paragraph(&[])` fabricated one line at the default font size instead of returning none, so `ensure_nonempty_paragraph`'s `is_empty()` early-returned and never synthesized the correct paragraph-mark-sized line. Fix: return an empty `LineLayout` for empty runs; the flow layer then sizes the empty line by the mark run's metrics (Word behaviour) — a key vertical-rhythm/page-count factor. **(4) Arial resolved to bundled Roboto (metrics too tall → wrong wrapping/pagination).** The pure resolver maps unbundled families to Roboto (correct for WASM/deterministic), but the shaper pushed that bundled name to parley even when the real family is installed, so a `system-fonts` build never used system Arial. Fix: `StyledRun` carries the originally-requested family (`w:rFonts`, theme-resolved); `ParleyShaper::pick_family` prefers it when the font collection actually has a face of that name (a system face under `system-fonts`, or a host-registered blob), else the bundled fallback — so a deterministic/WASM build (name absent from the collection) is byte-identical, while a desktop build shapes and paginates with real Arial/Times/etc. Folded into `paragraph_hash`. **(5) CJK line overlap (coordinator report — SDS 急救措施/消防措施).** Sub-single `auto` line spacing (`w:line="168" w:lineRule="auto"` = 0.70×) scaled the line box under the glyph line so consecutive CJK lines overprinted. Fix (`flow.rs line_constraints`): floor the `auto` multiple at 100% (LibreOffice does not compress `auto` below the single line); a genuine multiple ≥1.0 is untouched. **Corpus counts (LibreOffice oracle in parens): Sample 26→27 (26), demo 8→10 (8), Class notes 1→1 (1), SDS 17→23 (16), Medical 3→4→3 (4).** The **acceptance proof** (rendered): Sample page 1 = cover only, pages 2–3 = Table of Contents, page 4 = "1. Text Formatting & Typography", page 5 = "2. Heading Levels" — i.e. sections land on the exact pages the TOC lists (was: cover+TOC crammed on page 1). SDS 急救/消防 sections now read as separate non-overlapping lines matching LibreOffice. All 5 gates green; no golden changed (deterministic path is untouched — system-font preference and the sub-100% `auto` floor only alter the native `system-fonts` render). **Open (residual, not blocking content-per-page for the acceptance doc):** total counts still drift a little above the oracle (Sample +1, demo +2, SDS +7) from pre-existing fidelity gaps outside this change — image/table intrinsic sizing (demo is image/table-heavy), multi-column section geometry (SDS has 18 `continuous` column-change sections rendered single-column), and `lineRule="exact"` values smaller than the CJK glyph line (Word clips; LibreOffice grows — a follow-up must decide the oracle-matching rule without breaking Latin `exact` clipping). A stale `PAGE`/`NUMPAGES` value was observed in the SDS footer (field-resolution, separate from pagination). |
| P1F-COLUMNS | Multi-column section flow (`w:cols`) | Claude Code | Done | **Page-fidelity: newspaper-column flow.** `w:cols` was modeled but every section flowed as one full-width column, spreading the SDS over ~7 extra pages (23 vs LibreOffice 16). New `casual-doc-layout/src/columns.rs`: a column-aware, multi-section paginator (`paginate_columns`) that fills a section's columns in order — column 0 top-to-bottom, then column 1, … then the next page (Word's non-balanced order) — with content wrapped at the **column** width. `document_layout.rs` now partitions the body into one `SectionRun` per section (`build_section_runs`; each ends at the paragraph carrying its `w:sectPr`, final section body-level), flows each at its own column width via new `flow::build_galley_for_blocks`, and carries the page cursor across boundaries: a `nextPage`/`evenPage`/`oddPage`/default section starts a fresh page, a `continuous`/`nextColumn` section continues on the **same** page with its column band beginning just below the previous section's deepest content (the mid-page column-set change the SDS exercises — its multi-column sections are `continuous`). Paragraph line-splitting, keep-next groups, keep-lines, widow/orphan, and table-row split are reused from `paginate.rs` (helpers made `pub(crate)`); flow provenance mirrors the single-column paginator so `paginate_document` == the hand-wired single-column pipeline for a single-section doc (the `driver_equals_manual_wiring` regression still holds). The incremental/halt core of `paginate.rs` is untouched (the driver uses `paginate_columns` on full runs; incremental `repaginate` stays single-column). **Corpus counts (LibreOffice oracle in parens): SDS 23→18 (16), Sample 29→26 (26 ✓), demo 10 (8), Class notes 1 (1 ✓), Medical 4 (4 ✓).** SDS materially closer to 16 and now visually flows in two columns matching LibreOffice's label/content structure (rendered + compared page-for-page); Sample improved to the oracle exactly with cover=p1/TOC=p2 held. **Deferred (documented in `columns.rs`):** (a) per-column widths — the model carries only column count + space (not `w:equalWidth`/per-`w:col` widths), so unequal columns (the SDS's 3163/6447 split) render as N equal columns of the same total width; capturing specified widths needs a model+import+export extension and per-column reshaping; (b) last-page column balancing — columns fill in order and the section's final page is left unbalanced, so a following `continuous` section (which must start below all filled columns to avoid overlap) can be pushed to a new page where balancing would keep it (the residual SDS 18 vs 16); (c) column breaks collapse to page breaks (as in single-column flow) and a table in a multi-column section does not repeat header rows across columns; (d) per-section header/footer variants (bands still reserved once from the first section). 3 new column unit tests + all 5 gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy, workspace test, doc, wasm). |
| P1F-COLUMNS-2 | Unequal per-column widths + `w:cols/@w:sep` separator rule | Claude Code | Done | **Closes P1F-COLUMNS deferrals (a) per-column widths and the never-drawn separator.** **Model** (`model/v1/definitions.rs`, additive/strict serde): new `ColumnDef { width_twips, space_twips: Option }` (`w:col/@w:w`/`@w:space`); `SectionColumns` gains `equal_width: Option<bool>` (`w:cols/@w:equalWidth`) + `columns: Vec<ColumnDef>` (dropped `Copy`, still `Clone`). **Import** (`import/body.rs`): `w:cols/@w:equalWidth` parsed; a new `w:col` handler accumulates per-column widths/gaps (bounded to 64); `build_section` maps them (clamped). **Export** (`export/semantic.rs`): re-emits `w:equalWidth` and, when per-column defs exist, `w:cols` as a non-empty element wrapping one `w:col` each (else the self-closing form). **Layout** (`columns.rs`): `column_layout` places explicit unequal columns left-to-right at their true widths + per-`w:col` gaps (used when `!columns.is_empty() && equal_width != Some(true)`, else equal division); `flow_width` returns the **widest** column's width so the wide content column wraps at its true width (documented single-galley approximation — a narrow column wider than itself can overrun; true per-column reflow deferred). Separator: the column paginator now closes each page band and emits a `ColumnSeparator{x,top,bottom}` (new `page.rs` type + `Page.separators` field, off the single-column hot path) for every inter-column gap when `w:cols/@w:sep`; `compose.rs` paints each as a `PaintItem::Line` (~0.5pt black hairline centered in the gap). **Verify (rendered + LibreOffice oracle):** SDS unequal 3163/6447 columns now render narrow-left-label / wide-right-content matching LibreOffice (page-for-page), page count **19→17** (oracle 16; residual 1 page is the separate CJK line-metrics fix). The SDS declares no `w:sep`, so — correctly — no rule there; the separator is proven on `Sample Document.docx` (`equalWidth="1" sep="1"`), which draws a full-height vertical rule between its two equal columns (page 21, x=6120tw, pixel-continuous), page count held at **26** with cover=p1/TOC=p2. No regression: demo 10, Class notes 1, Medical 3 unchanged. 6 new tests (unequal division, equalWidth-true override, separator emitted/absent, import map, export round-trip) + all 5 gates green. **Remaining P1F-COLUMNS deferrals (b) last-page balancing, (c) column→page break collapse, (d) per-section header/footer variants still open; plus per-column galley reflow.** |
| P1F-VML-HR | Inline VML horizontal rule (`v:rect@o:hr`) + header/footer VML text-box positioning | Claude Code | Done | **Rendering fidelity (two VML bugs).** (1) Word's "Insert → Horizontal Line" (`<w:pict><v:rect o:hr="t" .../></w:pict>`) was dropped — the VML path handled only positioned shapes, so an inline `o:hr` (full-content-width grey line in its own paragraph) rendered as nothing. Now a first-class additive `InlineNode::HorizontalRule` (align `o:hralign`, `width_permille` `o:hrpct`, `thickness_emu` from `height` defaulting ~1.5pt, `color` from `fillcolor` defaulting grey `#A0A0A0`) is parsed (`vml.rs` `parse_hr` + `VmlHr`), imported (`body.rs` `vml_hr_segment`, before the generic shape path), laid out on its own line (`flow.rs` `hr_item`/`hr_line` → `text.rs` `InlineRule` on `Line::rules`, width/alignment resolved against content width), painted as a `PaintItem::Rect` (`compose.rs`), and exported back to `w:pict`/`v:rect@o:hr` (`semantic.rs`) — a model fixed point. (2) PR #135 forced **all** VML text boxes inline (to de-overlap body callouts), which stacked the SDS header's `修订日期`/`打印日期` date boxes vertically instead of side by side and over-reserved header height. Now a `v:textbox` in a header/footer part (`hf_root`) with a genuine absolute `margin-left`/`top` is placed as a float at its VML box (deferred from `exit_frame` to `commit_pict`, which knows the parsed position; a degenerate one falls back to inline), while **body** VML text boxes keep #135's inline behavior — so the header boxes sit horizontal without re-breaking the body de-overlap. Verified vs LibreOffice: Class notes grey rule under "Notes" (1 page, unchanged); SDS header dates side by side and page count 18 → 16 with body callouts (pages 0–3) intact. Tests: `o:hr` parse (+percent/align, +grey/thickness defaults), import→rule, header→float / body→inline, full-width + partial-width alignment layout, `o:hr` export round-trip. |

| P1F-VML-INLINE-SIZE | Inline VML image captures its authored CSS width/height | Claude Code | Done | **Rendering fidelity (`55-CURRENT-DOCX-FIDELITY-GAP-AUDIT.md` §8/P1, `body.rs::vml_segment` / `flow.rs::image_item`).** A genuinely inline `v:imagedata` (no absolute position/z-order, so `vml_is_floating` is false) always mapped to `Segment::Drawing { extent: None }`, even when the enclosing `v:shape@style` CSS carried an explicit `width`/`height` — `parse_vml_pict` already parsed both into `VmlPosition.width`/`.height` (twips), but `vml_segment`'s inline branch discarded them outright. Since `casual-doc-layout::flow::image_item` returns `None` (contributes nothing to the line) whenever `extent` is `None`, an inline VML image with a fully authored CSS box still silently vanished from layout despite importing without error. Fixed: the inline branch now converts `VmlPosition.width`/`.height` to EMU via the existing `vml_extent` helper (twips × 635 EMU/twip, the same conversion the floating/text-box paths already used) whenever **both** dimensions are present and positive, and only then; a box missing either dimension (as with a bare `width`-only style, still exercised by `vml_pict_image_becomes_a_drawing`) or a degenerate zero-size one still leaves `extent: None` rather than fabricating a bogus size. No model or layout changes — the fix is entirely within `vml_segment`'s existing inline-image arm. 1 new test (`vml_pict_inline_image_with_css_size_captures_extent`: `style="width:72pt;height:36pt"` → `Extent{914_400, 457_200}` EMU); the pre-existing width-only test and its now-clarified comment are unchanged. `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p casual-doc-import --all-features --locked` (207 passed), and `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` all green. |
| P1F-FOOTER-PAGE-FIELD | Footer PAGE/NUMPAGES resolve per page (was stale) | Claude Code | Done | **Rendering fidelity (stale field value).** The SDS Chinese footer showed a frozen `10 / 16` on every page: its page number lives in a positioned VML `v:textbox` (a `PAGE`/`NUMPAGES` complex `fldChar` sequence), which the importer models as an *anchored* `TextBox`. Anchored footer boxes are materialized by `place_floats` into `Page::anchored`, but `resolve_fields` ran *before* `place_floats` and never walked anchored content, so the box kept the cached result baked into the file. Fix: `resolve_in_fragment` now recurses into inline text boxes (`Line::text_boxes`), and a new `resolve_anchored_fields` pass — wired into `paginate_document` *after* `place_floats` — resolves `PAGE`/`NUMPAGES` inside `AnchorContent::TextBox` blocks (recursing cells + nested boxes). Field classification already tolerated `\* MERGEFORMAT` switches (leading keyword). Body/header/footer inline field handling unchanged (Sample pages pixel-identical). Verified vs LibreOffice: SDS footer now `1/16`, `2/16`, `3/16`, … `16/16`. Tests: `PAGE`/`NUMPAGES` in a footer text box resolve per page, a `\* MERGEFORMAT`-switched `PAGE` resolves, and a `PAGE` in an inline text box resolves. |

| P1F-RTL-SCRIPT | Consume paragraph base direction + per-script font slots in layout | Claude Code | Done | **Paint fidelity (F1), closes two of the three `55-…#7` gaps:** layout hardcoded `LineConstraints.rtl=false` and resolved every run's face from the ascii slot only, so `w:bidi` paragraphs laid out LTR and CJK/complex-script runs shaped in the Latin font at Latin weight/size. Now: (1) `flow.rs::line_constraints` derives `rtl` from the paragraph's effective `w:bidi` — and `cascade.rs::overlay_paragraph`, which had silently dropped the P1B-COV-PAR bidi/East-Asian toggles on cascade, now propagates them; (2) an RTL paragraph's direction-relative alignment resolves against the correct edge (`shape.rs::alignment` maps `Start`→`Right`/`End`→`Left` under `rtl`, since parley 0.11 exposes no API to force a base level and would mis-detect a Latin-charactered RTL paragraph as LTR); (3) new `script.rs` (single source of truth for the East-Asian/complex Unicode ranges — the shaper's `is_cjk_scalar` now delegates to it) partitions a run carrying per-script formatting into script spans, and `flow.rs::push_styled_runs` resolves each span against its own `w:rFonts` slot (`cascade.rs::requested_font_family_for` reads `font_ref_east_asia`/`font_ref_cs`, falling back to ascii, with `font_hint` disambiguating neutrals) per ECMA-376 §17.3.2.26; a `w:cs` span also shapes with `bold_complex`/`italic_complex`/`size_complex_half_points` (`w:bCs`/`w:iCs`/`w:szCs`). The split is gated on the run declaring per-script intent (explicit `w:eastAsia`/`w:cs` font, a CS bold/italic/size, or an EA/complex `w:rFonts@hint`), so the Latin corpus stays byte-identical (all existing goldens/tests unchanged). **Explicitly deferred (still open, recorded in `55-…#7`):** full bidi *visual reordering* of mixed LTR/RTL runs within a line (parley owns per-run bidi; opendoc does not reorder and cannot force parley's base level), vertical writing modes, and theme-only per-script resolution for a run with no explicit `w:eastAsia`/`w:cs`. Layout only — no model/import/export change. 9 new tests (`script.rs` classification/partition units; `flow.rs` line_constraints-derives-rtl, mixed Latin+CJK slot selection, complex-script cs face/bold/size, Latin fast-path-unchanged; `shape.rs` alignment mapping + RTL-right-aligns-start). Gates: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`, `cargo test -p casual-doc-layout --all-features --locked` (305 pass), and `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` all green. |

| P1F-FLOAT-SQUARE-2 | Page-relative float exclusion descends into table cells | Claude Code | Done | **Closes one `P1F-FLOAT-SQUARE`/`P1F-FLOAT-CROSS-PARAGRAPH` residual (`55-…` §3, `60-…` remaining-work-order #3): "cross-paragraph exclusion does not yet descend into tables, text boxes, or running bands."** `document_layout.rs::paragraph_float_exclusions` collected exclusion targets only from top-level `page.placed` `BlockFragment::Paragraph` entries, so a page/margin-relative float whose resolved band overlapped a paragraph nested inside a table cell had its exclusion silently dropped — `ctx.paragraph_float_exclusions` is already threaded into `flow_table`'s cell recursion (same `FlowCtx`), but the map was never keyed by any cell paragraph's `NodeId`. Fix: two new recursive helpers (`collect_cell_paragraph_rects`, `collect_block_paragraph_rects`) walk a placed `TableRow`'s cells — skipping `w:vMerge` continuation cells, honoring `w:tcMar`/`w:vAlign` content offset and per-block height stacking, exactly mirroring `compose::compose_fragment`/`compose_blocks`'s paint geometry — to recover each nested paragraph's absolute page rect, recursing through nested tables to arbitrary depth. Only cells inside a genuine top-level **body** table (`body_table_ids`, mirroring the existing `body_order` top-level-paragraph guard) are descended into, so note/header/footer tables sharing the same placed-fragment list are not misattributed. The existing three-pass fixed point, conservative-envelope fallback, and per-side interval union are unchanged — this only widens *which paragraphs* can receive a computed exclusion. **Scope:** table cells only; a float's exclusion still does not reach paragraphs inside a floating text box or a header/footer running band (both flow through an independent `FlowCtx` with `paragraph_float_exclusions: None` — `flow_anchored_text_box`/`flow_running_blocks`), and tight/through contour wrapping remains deferred. New test `margin_float_excludes_a_paragraph_nested_in_a_table_cell` (`document_layout.rs::cross_paragraph_float_tests`) asserts a margin-relative float excludes intersecting lines of a paragraph inside a one-cell table and restores full cell width below it; verified to fail (no exclusion applied) against the pre-fix collection loop before confirming it passes with the fix. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p casual-doc-layout --all-features --locked` (all green), and `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` all green. |

Also made the page surface the explicit focus owner (`tabindex=0`), kept link chips from stealing focus, gated editing keystrokes away from chrome controls, and changed document color/shading controls from per-frame `input` edits to final-choice `change` commits so picker drags do not flood repagination/repaint. Column resizing now exposes layout-derived internal column-edge handles for regular tables and the web editor draws Word-style resize hit targets with a cheap preview line, committing one undoable width transaction on pointer release to avoid frame-rate regressions during drag.
