| P1F-REVIEW-MARKUP-VIEW | Read-only review-markup render view policy | Claude Code | In progress | **Galley capability landed** (design `docs/93`): `casual-doc-layout::flow` gains `ReviewView { Editing (default), Markup }` on `FlowCtx` and a fresh (non-cached) `build_galley_markup` / `build_galley_with_report_view` entry — under `Markup`, the revision arm keeps + strikes deletions/moveFrom (shown, author-colored) and underlines + author-colors insertions/moveTo, and a comment-range stack highlights the runs between `CommentRangeStart`/`End`, all via the existing `StyledRun` color/decoration/highlight fields (no render-crate change). Author→color is a case-folded FNV-1a hash over a palette byte-identical to the webapp overlay (`REVIEW_AUTHOR_PALETTE`, P1G-REVIEW-048) so an author reads the same color in both, with the same `#5f6368` unattributed fallback; presentation-only, never persisted. `Editing` is the default so every existing call site, the wasm caret byte space, and the incremental cache path are unchanged (`Markup` is uncached and never fed to caret/selection). Verified: a `flow` unit test (editing view drops the deletion / no highlight; markup view shows the struck deletion, underlines+colors the insertion, highlights the comment range, same author→same color) + full `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked`, strict clippy, `cargo doc -D warnings`, wasm32. **Driver plumbing landed:** `paginate_document_view(document, shaper, ReviewView)` threads the view through the full `paginate_document` pipeline (section runs → `push_section_run` → `build_body_galley` → `build_galley_for_blocks_inner`, plus the float-exclusion passes), so `ReviewView::Markup` produces a complete **read-only paginated layout** that shows struck deletions. `paginate_document` is `paginate_document_view(Editing)` (unchanged); the incremental cached path stays `Editing`-only. An end-to-end `document_layout` test confirms the markup layout paints struck deletion glyphs the editing layout drops. **Consumer landed — the view is now user-visible.** `casual-doc-wasm` adds `setShowChanges(on)` / `showingChanges` + a `markup_layout: Option<PaginatedLayout>` field built via `paginate_document_view(Markup)`; `renderPage`/`pageCount` read the markup layout while it is on (caret/selection/hit-test always read the editing `layout`, so it stays a read-only preview). The webapp adds a "Show changes" item in the View app-menu (view.showChanges) that flips it and re-renders (`renderAll` syncs `setShowChanges` each pass so a preview always reflects the latest document). Verified: a wasm toggle test + a Playwright `show-changes-toggle.spec.mjs` (author a tracked deletion → toggle on renders the markup canvas with the pressed state and no console errors → toggle off). **Status: Done** for the core view (`Editing`/`Markup`). Later slices: `Original`/`Final` accept/reject previews. Closes the docs/55 §11 / docs/60 §11 "review semantics have no view policy" residual (the view P1G- Accepted design closing the docs/55 §11 / docs/60 §11 "review semantics have no view policy" residual (the view P1G-REVIEW-036 deferred). The native galley hard-codes `FinalWithMarkup` with **no** presentation: insertions/moveTo render as ordinary accepted text (no author color/underline), deletions/moveFrom are dropped to zero width (struck text not shown), comment ranges get no highlight. It can't be a paint-only fix because the webapp editor rasterizes the **same** galley via `renderPage` and already draws per-author markup in a DOM overlay (P1G-REVIEW-048), so baking styling into the shared galley would double-mark the editor and change its byte space (which ~15 wasm caret sites depend on staying zero-width). Design: add a `ReviewView { Editing (default, unchanged), Markup (read-only) }` parameter threaded through `build_galley*` into `FlowCtx` and **folded into the galley cache key**; under `Markup` the deletion/moveFrom arm keeps+strikes content and insertions/moveTo get author color + underline and comment ranges a highlight, all via the existing `GlyphRun` color/decoration/highlight fields (no render-crate change); author→color reuses the webapp FNV-1a 10-hue palette (presentation-only, never persisted). `Markup` is a separate read-only galley reached only through a read-only entry, never fed to caret/selection/hit-test, so the editor byte space and incremental halt are untouched; only the native fidelity/PNG viewer requests it. No model/import/export/edit-op change. Implementation (PR2) follows on its own branch; `Original`/`Final` accept/reject previews are a later PR3. |

| P1G-REVIEW-041 | Non-blocking comment-hit caret placement | Claude | Done | The commented-range highlight span (`.review-comment-marker`) no longer carries a click handler that stops propagation and resets the selection to the entire comment range. `onPointerDown`'s hit-test remains the sole source of truth for every click, including one that lands directly on the marker; card expansion/highlight is now a non-blocking side effect derived from the resulting caret (`syncActiveReviewCommentToCaret`) instead of driving the caret. | P1G-REVIEW-042 | Suggesting-mode command-routing gate (REVIEW-GAP-004 slice) | Claude Code | Done | Closes only the REVIEW-GAP-004 portion of P1G-REVIEW-037: `runToolbarEdit`'s fail-closed Suggesting-mode gate is factored into a shared `blockUntrackedInSuggesting()` check and applied to every remaining direct `runEdit`/`runNodeEdit` mutation path that had none — table style/width/properties/formula/sort/merge/split/insert/row/column/delete, ribbon and command-palette Insert Table, ribbon and command-palette Restart Numbering, Find/Replace apply (single and All), document properties, page setup, and a rich-paste fallback path that could reach `pasteRichRuns` untracked when no plain-text extraction was possible. `runNodeEdit` (every call site is a table/list structural op) now fails closed unconditionally. Rebasing onto the concurrent context-aware-menus work (P1G-REVIEW-037-adjacent) found its right-click table mutations already UI-disabled under Suggesting via `structuralEnabled`, but their `run` closures still called bare `runEdit`; the same `{ gate: true }` was added there too so the block holds at the mutation layer, not only the menu's enabled state. None of these operations gain tracked-revision representation (REVIEW-GAP-009 stays open); they are blocked in Suggesting mode with the existing "This command cannot be tracked yet; switch to Editing to apply it" status instead of silently mutating. Comment add/reply/resolve/delete, revision accept/reject/accept-all/reject-all, and Undo/Redo remain intentionally mode-independent. Verified with 23 frontend unit tests and a new dedicated Playwright spec (`suggesting-mode-gate.spec.mjs`, 3 tests) plus the full existing 55-test browser suite (58 total), all passing locally after building the WASM package from this branch; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed; no Rust was changed. |
| P1G-REVIEW-043 | Typed review query API (`listComments`/`listRevisions`/`commentThread`) | Claude | Done | Closes REVIEW-GAP-022. `casual-doc-wasm` adds three first-class typed query methods alongside the existing combined `reviewSummary()` (kept working, unchanged, for back-compat): `listComments() -> String` (JSON array of `{id, author, initials, date, resolved, paraId, parentParaId, text, anchor?}`), `listRevisions() -> String` (JSON array of `{id, kind, author, date, groupId?, groupKind?, revisionId?, text, anchor, movePair?, formattingDelta?}`), and `commentThread(comment) -> Result<String, JsValue>` (one thread's root + every descendant reply, root-first, resolving to the same thread from any member id via the same parent-chain walk `deleteComment` already uses; errs on an unknown/invalid id). Each JSON shape is backed by a named, `serde`-derived Rust struct (`ReviewCommentSummaryJson`/`ReviewRevisionSummaryJson`), not an ad hoc `serde_json::json!` literal, so the schema is compile-time checked and documented rather than implicit. `webapp/src/main.js` now calls the typed methods everywhere it previously hand-parsed `reviewSummary()`'s JSON. Verified with 4 new WASM tests (typed list/thread contract, cross-checked against `reviewSummary`'s legacy output, nested reply-to-a-reply thread resolution, unknown/invalid id errors), the full workspace test/doc-test suite, strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, 23 frontend unit tests, the release WASM build, and all 55 Playwright tests (including the full `review-margin` suite exercising the rewritten `main.js` call sites end-to-end in a real browser). |
| P1G-REVIEW-046 | Real read-only Viewing mode (REVIEW-GAP-014) | Claude Code | Done | Closes REVIEW-GAP-014, the "real Viewing mode" portion of P1G-REVIEW-037. `setReviewMode` previously collapsed every non-suggesting value to `editing`; it now models all three modes (`editing`/`suggesting`/`viewing`) with `viewing` fully read-only per docs/68 §"Suggesting mode" ("no Operation reaches apply"). Enforcement mirrors P1G-REVIEW-042's fail-closed pattern: a single shared `blockMutationInViewing()` guard is checked at the top of all three mutation entry points (`runEdit`, `runToolbarEdit`, `runNodeEdit`), so every mutating path fails closed — typing, backspace/delete, Enter, paste (and its rich/IME-commit variants), toolbar and paragraph formatting, list/indent ops, table style/structure/formatting ops, undo/redo, and comment/revision decisions (add/reply/resolve/delete, accept/reject/accept-all/reject-all) — without relying on any individual command or menu item being disabled. `cut`/`paste` additionally fail closed up front so no clipboard side effect or partial execution occurs. Non-mutating reader capabilities remain: caret/selection, scroll, Find (search only), and copy (copy writes to the clipboard but never mutates the document). UI: the visible ribbon toggle became a three-button segmented control (`#reviewModeControl`, `data-review-mode="editing|suggesting|viewing"`) reusing the existing `reviewModeButtons`/`setReviewMode` wiring rather than a parallel system; a read-only `#viewingBanner` shows the state, and ⌘⇧E now cycles Editing→Suggesting→Viewing for keyboard access. Verified with a new `viewing-mode-gate.spec.mjs` (2 specs: one proving typing/deletion/paste/formatting/table-insert are all blocked with the engine word count unchanged and the typed markers unfindable; one proving selection extends and a synthetic copy event still yields clipboard text) plus the full 66-test Playwright suite green after building the WASM from this branch, 23 frontend unit tests, and the four existing mode-switching specs (`suggesting-mode-gate`, `context-menu`, `review-margin`, `rich-paste-suggesting`) updated to drive the new segmented control via a shared `setReviewMode` fixture helper. No Rust changed. |

The webapp's Settings panel gained real Name/Initials fields wired to this API, replacing the legacy hidden `#reviewAuthor` input read from the permanently-hidden Review panel (deleted, not left as a second path). | P1G-REVIEW-045 | Rich paste preserves formatting at a collapsed caret in Suggesting mode (REVIEW-GAP-008 slice) | Claude | Done | Narrows, but does not close, REVIEW-GAP-008. `webapp/src/main.js`'s new `pasteTrackedRichRuns` handles a rich paste at a collapsed caret whose clipboard runs are all one paragraph: it chains one tracked `doc.suggestStyledInsert` per run under one gesture (`typingSessionForKey`), which the existing paragraph-snapshot typing-tick coalescing (docs 82 §3) merges into one review card and one Undo step — so pasted bold/italic/underline/strike/color/highlight/size/font now survive instead of flattening to one plain-text tracked insertion. `pasteRichRunsJson` falls back to the prior flattened-plain-text/reject behavior exactly when this does not apply: multi-paragraph clipboard content (any run with `paragraphBreak`) stays rejected outright, and a rich paste that also needs to replace an existing selection stays a flattened single-format tracked replacement — no tracked multi-run *replacement* group exists yet, since `RevisionGroupKind::Replacement` requires exactly one deletion plus one insertion. A run's `href` is still not carried into the tracked insertion (unchanged from before). No Rust changed. Verifying the fix surfaced a separate, pre-existing gap (REVIEW-GAP-030, recorded in doc 81): the Home ribbon's format-toolbar reflection does not see into `InlineNode::Revision`-wrapped runs, so selecting formatted text inside *any* pending suggestion (not specific to paste) under-reports its format; the new test asserts formatting through `copyRichRuns` (which does walk into revisions correctly) rather than the toolbar. Verified with 3 new Playwright specs (`rich-paste-suggesting.spec.mjs`: single-paragraph rich paste preserves per-run formatting as one card/one Undo step, multi-paragraph paste stays rejected, selection-replacing rich paste stays a flattened tracked replacement) plus the full existing 61-test Playwright suite (64 total), 23 frontend unit tests, the release WASM build, and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` (no Rust changed, so Clippy/`cargo test` were unaffected but re-run clean regardless). |
| P1G-REVIEW-047 | Comment/reply edit-delete and comment-to-revision overlap | Claude Code | Done | Closes REVIEW-GAP-011 and advances REVIEW-GAP-012 (docs/81). `casual-doc-wasm` adds `updateComment(id, text)` — edits a thread root's or any reply's body text in place, preserving `para_id`/`parent_para_id` threading (a reply stays a reply and export's `w14:paraId`/`w15:paraIdParent` join keys survive reopen), resolved state, author identity, and anchor markers — and `deleteReply(id)` — removes one reply plus its nested sub-replies, failing closed on a thread-root id so `deleteComment` alone owns whole-thread deletion. Both are one undoable `UpdateReviewState` action. For REVIEW-GAP-012, `revisionThread(revisionId)` reports the comments whose anchor ranges overlap a tracked change in the shared final-with-markup byte space (a half-open/zero-width overlap rule), and the review sidebar threads those comments read-only beneath an expanded revision card; this invents no comment↔revision model link and changes no DOCX comment-ownership semantics — a first-class reply composer on a change and commenting on zero-width deletion content stay deferred. Webapp: per-reply Edit/Delete controls in the review sidebar. Verified with `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, strict all-target/all-feature workspace Clippy, 7 new `casual-doc-wasm` unit tests (90 total in that crate), the full `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` gate green, `webapp/build.sh` release WASM, 23 frontend unit tests, and the full 62-test Playwright suite (including a new reply edit/delete `review-margin` regression). |
| P1G-REVIEW-049 | Keyboard-accessible move-card navigation with precise page labels (REVIEW-GAP-016) | Claude | Done | Closes REVIEW-GAP-016 (the move half; P1G-REVIEW-036 already closed the formatting-delta half). A tracked-move review card's "From" and "To" ends were static, non-interactive `<span>`s reading a generic "Original location"/"New location". They are now keyboard-focusable `<button>`s (`reviewMoveEndButton` in `webapp/src/main.js`): each shows the precise page its end sits on (`Page N`, computed from the same `reviewRangeClientRect` range geometry the sidebar already uses to place cards, falling back to the generic label only when geometry is unavailable), carries a descriptive `aria-label`/`title` ("Go to the moved text's original/new location (page N)"), and on activation jumps the caret/selection to that end (via a new `navigateToReviewAnchor` helper — the move source and destination resolve to their own distinct anchors: the destination range selects the relocated text, the zero-width source collapses to a caret) and scrolls it to centre. The card's keydown handler now ignores Enter/Space that originate on an inner control, so the nav buttons (and the existing Accept/Reject buttons) own their own keyboard without also toggling the card's expansion. No API/Rust change — `listRevisions()`'s `movePair` plus the item's source/destination anchors were already exposed (PR #248); this is purely a sidebar rendering/interaction change. Verified by extending `review-margin.spec.mjs`'s tracked-move spec (asserts both ends are role=button with precise `Page N` text, that clicking the destination selects a range while the source collapses to a caret, and that accept/reject/undo still work) plus the full 69-test Playwright suite, 23 frontend unit tests, the release WASM build, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and strict all-target/all-feature Clippy (both clean; no Rust changed). |
| P1G-REVIEW-046 | Unify the comment column into the canvas scroll context (single scroll owner) | Claude Code | Done | The comments/suggestions column and the canvas were two sibling `overflow:auto` scroll containers kept in lockstep by bidirectional JS scroll-sync, which rendered the canvas scrollbar *between* the canvas and the comments and let comment cards drift/lag against their anchored text under momentum scroll. Adopts the Google Docs single-scroll model: `#reviewSidebar` now lives *inside* `#viewport` as an absolutely-positioned right-margin layer (`webapp/editor.html`), `.review-sidebar` is no longer a scroll container (`webapp/src/style.css`: `position:absolute; top:0; right:12px`, no `overflow`), and `#pages` reserves the column's width via `.viewport.has-review-sidebar { padding-right }` so pages stay centered-ish and the single `.viewport` scrollbar sits past the comments. The bidirectional scroll-sync listeners, the `syncingReviewScroll`/`suppressReviewSidebarScroll` plumbing, the sidebar-body-height mirroring, and the per-scroll-frame re-render are deleted (`webapp/src/main.js`); cards, positioned in document-scroll coordinates, now ride the canvas natively so their card-to-anchor gap is scroll-invariant. Also adds one thin/overlay/quiet scrollbar treatment applied globally to every `overflow:auto` surface (`--scrollbar-thumb`/`-hover` tokens, `scrollbar-width:thin` + translucent `::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb`), replacing the default chunky OS bars (only the sidebar had a bespoke hide before). Verified: 23 frontend unit tests, `webapp/build.sh` (release WASM), and the full Playwright suite (63 tests before rebase; re-run after) including a new `review-scroll-unification.spec.mjs` (2 tests) that proves the sidebar is a non-scrolling descendant of `#viewport`, the card-to-anchor gap stays within 2px across a >200px canvas scroll, and the column sits in the viewport's right margin; the existing `review-margin` suite passes unchanged. Advances but does **not** close REVIEW-GAP-020: removing the per-scroll rebuild reduces scroll jank, but the retained/virtualized item model is still open (tracked under P1G-REVIEW-039). |
| P1G-REVIEW-048 | Per-author review color + attribution tooltip (REVIEW-GAP-015 slice) | Claude Code | Done | Mostly closes REVIEW-GAP-015: every reviewer previously shared the same green/red overlay colors with no author/date tooltip, so multiple authors were visually indistinguishable. The webapp now assigns each distinct author a deterministic, stable color from a fixed 10-hue palette, keyed by an FNV-1a hash of the author's name (the stable identity the `listComments`/`listRevisions` projection exposes; **presentation only, never persisted** into the model — the approach docs/68 §50 specifies). One author renders in one color across all their changes and comments; different authors differ. Applied to (1) inline tracked-insertion/deletion markers, (2) comment highlight/anchor markers, and (3) the review-margin sidebar card avatar chip, all via a per-element `--review-author-color` CSS custom property consumed by `color-mix`/`background` (insertion = author-colored underline, deletion = author-colored tint + underline — the existing fill-vs-underline distinction preserved, just recolored). Adds a native `title` attribution tooltip (author · change-type · date for a revision; author · state · date for a comment) on hover of both the inline marker and its sidebar card — reusing the editor's existing `title` tooltip pattern, no new chrome. Revision markers become `pointer-events: auto` so the tooltip shows on hover; caret placement inside a tracked change is unaffected because pointerdown still bubbles to the page's coordinate-based hit-testing (the same pattern the comment marker already uses — REVIEW-GAP-005). Degrades cleanly: a single author looks unchanged-clean; an unattributed change falls back to "You" with a neutral non-palette grey. **JS/CSS only — no Rust/WASM/model change** (the projection already exposed the author name). Deferred, still open under REVIEW-GAP-015: multi-letter avatar initials, and an automated light/dark contrast gate (palette chosen for legibility over the white canvas and as a white-on-color chip in both themes, but not yet asserted by a test). Verified with 23 frontend unit tests (`npm run test:unit`) and the full Playwright suite — **71 tests, all passing** — including a new `per-author-color.spec.mjs` (2 tests: two authors' comments render in two distinct, self-consistent colors with attribution on both markers and cards; two authors' tracked insertions render in two distinct colors and show change attribution on hover), after building the WASM package from this branch (`webapp/build.sh`). The new spec uses the shared `setReviewMode` fixture helper introduced by P1G-REVIEW-046's three-state segmented control. |
| P1G-REVIEW-049 | Toolbar reflects formatting inside pending revisions (REVIEW-GAP-030) | Claude Code | Done | Closes REVIEW-GAP-030, the toolbar-reflection gap surfaced while verifying P1G-REVIEW-045. `casual_doc_edit::run_properties_in_range` (selection reflection) and `caret_run_properties` (collapsed-caret reflection) previously matched only a top-level `InlineNode::Run` via `run_segments`, silently skipping any run wrapped in a pending tracked `Revision` — so the Home ribbon's `#bold`/`#italic`/etc. stayed unpressed over suggested text even though `copyRichRuns` confirmed the model was correct. Both now walk a shared `flatten_run_segments` that descends into final-with-markup-contributing `Revision`/`Hyperlink`/`Sdt` wrappers with cumulative offsets aligned to `inline_text_len`, mirroring `walk_inlines_rich`. `format_state`/`caret_format` and the `selectionFormat`/`formatAt` WASM path built on `effective_run_properties_in_range` now reflect formatting inside a suggestion. The editing/split paths keep `run_segments` (top-level runs) because revision-aware splitting is separate (REVIEW-GAP-007). Verified with `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, strict all-target/all-feature workspace Clippy, a new `casual-doc-edit` regression (`format_state`/`caret_format` bold inside a pending insertion, plus mixed-selection and plain-text sanity), a new `casual-doc-wasm` `effective_run_properties_in_range` regression, the full `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` gate green, `webapp/build.sh` release WASM, 23 frontend unit tests, and the full 67-test Playwright suite including a new `#bold`-reflects-a-suggestion test in `rich-paste-suggesting.spec.mjs`. |
| P1G-REVIEW-050 | Review sidebar virtualization + retained item model (REVIEW-GAP-020) | Claude Code | Done | Builds on the single-scroll-owner sidebar (P1G-REVIEW-046) to add the retained/virtualized item model REVIEW-GAP-020 still lacked. Before this, every full render `replaceChildren()`'d the whole list and mounted **every** comment/revision card into the DOM at once, so a large review set allocated hundreds of card elements regardless of what was on screen and discarded/rebuilt all of them on each edit. Now a full render (content edit / resize only) computes each card's stacked document-scroll position once into a module-level `reviewLayout` and delegates mounting to `mountReviewWindow`, which attaches only the cards whose position falls inside a viewport ± `REVIEW_WINDOW_OVERSCAN` band and detaches the rest. A plain scroll runs `mountReviewWindow` via an rAF-debounced `scheduleReviewWindow` listener that windows the *precomputed* layout — no review-payload parse, no geometry recompute, no card rebuild — so the mounted-card count stays viewport-bounded no matter how many review items exist. Cards are retained in `reviewCardCache` keyed by a content signature (`reviewCardSignature`): an unchanged collapsed card is reused without rebuild or re-measure across edits; the active/expanded card and the composer always rebuild so their live controls (resolve/delete, reply composer, related-comment thread, focus) stay correct. Card heights (needed for exact stacking) are measured once per signature, batched (all new cards mounted off-view, all heights read in one layout, then detached) and cached, so a large set is not re-measured on every edit — card width is a fixed 340px, so height is viewport-independent. Positioning is byte-for-byte the same stacking the non-virtualized code produced (anchor top, pushed to clear the card above). Caches are cleared on document open and when the sidebar hides/empties. **Still open (kept Partial in docs/81):** a full render still calls `listComments`/`listRevisions` and recomputes anchor geometry per edit/resize (per-model-revision inventory caching to skip the edit-time re-parse is future work), and no formal 100/1,000-item wall-clock perf-threshold CI gate was added. Verified with a new `review-sidebar-virtualization.spec.mjs` on the 14-page sample document (a comment near the top and one ~9000px down; scrolled to the far comment the top card is proven **detached** from the DOM and the mounted count stays < 6, and scrolling back **remounts** the top card while the far card detaches — confirmed to fail if windowing is disabled), plus the full 75-test Playwright suite green (run against a private port to avoid a concurrent agent's server on 8099) after `webapp/build.sh`, 23 frontend unit tests, and the existing `review-margin`/`context-menu` suites unchanged. No Rust changed. |

| P1G-LIST-CHECKBOX-029 | Checklist authoring (checkable list items) | Claude Code | Done | Closes the "checklist" item of docs/67 list-lifecycle row 5. **Representation:** a checklist is a bullet-style list whose marker glyph is a checkbox — `☐` (U+2610) unchecked / `☒` (U+2612) checked; a per-item checked state *is* which of two lazily-created numbering definitions the item uses, so it round-trips to DOCX as an ordinary bullet list (Word shows checkbox bullets) with **no new model field** and is detected session-independently from the marker glyph (`checklist_state_of_instance`), so an imported/reopened checklist stays recognized and clickable. `☒` (not `☑`) is the checked glyph because the `☐`/`☒` ballot-box family is what `sample.docx` uses and what the covering symbol font is provisioned for (P1G-SHELL-THEME-002); detection still accepts `☑`. **Click-to-toggle (the part #260 couldn't do — markers had no click target):** the layout now tags injected marker glyph runs (`GlyphRun.is_marker`, additive/serde-skipped) and exposes their precise page-local rect via `LayoutSnapshot::marker_rects`; `casual-doc-wasm` adds `checklistMarkers()` (JSON `{node,page,x,y,w,h,checked}` per item), `toggleChecklistItem(node)`, `checkboxStateAt(node)`, and extends `toggleList(…, "checklist")`/`listStyleAt`→`"checklist"`. The webapp paints a transparent, clickable overlay over each engine-drawn checkbox (model-as-truth, mirroring the review-marker/table-handle overlay pattern) whose pointerdown toggles the item through `runNodeEdit` — **gated: blocked in Viewing and Suggesting**, consistent with the other list edits (a checkbox toggle has no tracked-revision representation). New items start unchecked. The webapp also re-provisions the covering symbol font after a checklist is created (the `☐`/`☒` glyphs are new coverage the on-open font pass never saw), so they render instead of a `.notdef` box. **Authoring:** a Paragraph-group **Checklist** toolbar toggle (`#checkList`); Enter/Tab/indent/empty-exit lifecycle reuses the existing bullet-list machinery unchanged. Model/layout/wasm/webapp only — no importer/exporter change (it exports as a bullet list already). Verified: 2 new `casual-doc-wasm` tests (create/toggle/reflect/off; export→reopen round-trip detected purely from the glyph — 92 crate tests), full `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` (0 failures) + strict all-target/all-feature Clippy + `cargo fmt --check` + `RUSTDOCFLAGS=-D warnings cargo doc` + `wasm32-unknown-unknown` check all green, 26 frontend unit tests, `webapp/build.sh`, and a new `checklist.spec.mjs` (create → click the marker to toggle checked/unchecked → toggle the list off; and a Viewing-mode-blocks-the-toggle spec) plus the full browser suite. Real-browser verified end-to-end: creating a checklist draws `☐`, clicking it flips to `☒` and back with the model state following. |
| P1G-PASTE-029 | Structured internal paste preserves tables and lists | Claude Code | Done | Closes the internal half of docs/67 clipboard row 7: an OpenDoc-to-OpenDoc copy of a table or list paragraphs now survives paste as real structure instead of flattening to text. **`casual-doc-edit`:** new general `InsertBlocks`/`DeleteBlocks` operation pair (each the other's exact inverse) splices a sequence of top-level blocks into a container (body/cell/SDT), the primitive behind block paste; unit tests cover the splice, its inverse round-trip (undo/redo), and out-of-range/empty rejection. **`casual-doc-wasm`:** `copyStructured` serializes the top-level body blocks a selection touches as the model's own `BlockNode` JSON — but only when at least one is a table or a list paragraph and every touched block is a paragraph/table (else returns `""` so plain prose keeps the flat rich-run path, and the existing tracked-paste path is untouched); `pasteStructured` reconstructs the fragment at a collapsed body-paragraph caret with fresh ids for every node, sanitizing inline content to runs/tabs/breaks/hyperlinks (exotic inline objects — drawings/fields/comments/bookmarks — are dropped rather than duplicated with dangling references), splitting the caret paragraph when mid-way, and committing one undoable `SplitParagraph`+`InsertBlocks` action. **`webapp`:** the internal clipboard marker now carries `{ blocks, runs }` (the flat runs ride along as the fallback); paste routes a structured fragment through `pasteStructured` in Editing mode, falling back to the flat runs when the engine declines (a range selection, or a caret inside a table cell) or in Suggesting mode (structural paste has no tracked representation — GAP-009). **Still flattening (noted in docs/67):** external HTML tables/lists, images (no insert-image path yet), fragment content inside cells, and cross-document numbering/media remap. Verified with 2 `casual-doc-edit` regressions, 3 `casual-doc-wasm` regressions (table reconstruct with fresh ids + undo; list numbering preserved; plain prose declines), the full workspace test suite, strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, `cargo fmt --check`, 23 frontend unit tests, `webapp/build.sh`, and 2 new Playwright specs (`structured-paste.spec.mjs`: a copied table pastes back as a real table — the contextual Table ribbon activates at the destination and undoes once; a copied numbered list pastes into prose keeping its numbering) plus the full 76-test browser suite. |

| P1G-A11Y-001 | Model-derived read-only accessibility tree for the canvas document (docs/67 row 9) | Claude | Done | Baseline for the accessibility half of docs/67 row 9. The document paints to a canvas, so its content was opaque to screen readers. `casual-doc-wasm` gains `accessibilityTree()` — a **read-only** JSON projection (typed `A11yBlockJson` enum, internally `kind`-tagged like the review query API) of the document's structure in order: headings (with 1-based level), paragraphs, list items (with an `ordered` flag), and tables (a grid of cell plain-text). The model stays the source of truth — this is a projection, not a hidden DOM editor (docs/67 Open Risks). **Deferred (row 9 remainder):** live-region status announcements (mode/save/comment), caret/reading-cursor semantics, table header (`th`/scope) semantics, AT-driven *editing*, and the entire **touch** interaction layer. Verified with `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, a new `casual-doc-wasm` unit test asserting the projection exposes the corpus's level-1 heading + paragraphs + a table and that toggling a plain paragraph to a bullet reclassifies it as an unordered list item (the full `cargo test --workspace --all-features` gate green), 23 frontend unit tests, and a new `accessibility-tree.spec.mjs` (3 specs: the off-screen tree exposes heading role/level + paragraphs + tables and is read-only/out of tab order; a bullet list renders as a real `ul`/`li`; the tree stays in sync when text is typed) plus the full Playwright suite — **82 tests, all passing** — after `webapp/build.sh`. |

| P1G-CLIPBOARD-IME-002 | Rich clipboard (paragraphs/runs/links) + IME live preedit | Claude Code | In review | Doc 67 Shell UX Implementation PR 2, closing the P0 "Native clipboard fidelity" and "IME live preedit" gaps (doc 58's noted deferral). **`casual-doc-wasm`:** new `copyRichRuns`/`pasteRichRuns` cross the JS boundary as one JSON string (a bridge payload, not a new engine type) covering paragraph text, run formatting (bold/italic/underline/strike/size/color/highlight/vertAlign/font), and hyperlinks; `copyRichRuns` walks paragraph inlines (modeled on `paragraph_links`/`collect_block_text`), falling back to existing plain-text extraction for tables/lists/exotic inlines (never silently dropped, only unstyled); `pasteRichRuns` builds one `InsertText`/`FormatText`/`SetHyperlink`/`SplitParagraph` batch and commits it via the existing `apply_action_caret` grouping, so a multi-run paste is one undo step. The round-trip test caught a real pre-existing bug in `casual-doc-edit`'s `insert_text`: inserting immediately after a trailing hyperlink (or any non-Run inline) at a paragraph edge silently merged into a distant preceding run instead of the correct position (`<=`/`>=` adjacency checks let the insert jump across the gap); fixed to require exact adjacency and to insert a fresh run at the correct top-level position otherwise. **`webapp/`:** new `src/clipboard.mjs` (`runsToHtml`, `htmlToRuns`, base64 marker embed/extract) builds `text/html` with a leading `<!--opendoc-clipboard-runs:…-->` comment carrying the exact `copyRichRuns` JSON for a lossless same-origin round-trip; an external paste (no marker) is parsed via `DOMParser` and a sanitizing walker (`b/i/u/s/sup/sub/a[href]`, best-effort inline color, block elements as paragraph breaks; `script`/`style` content skipped). `compositionupdate` (previously unhandled) now paints a live `.ime-preedit` overlay at the caret via the existing per-page overlay/`caretRect` infrastructure, removed on `compositionend`; the document is still only mutated on commit. Verified with `cargo fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -D warnings`/`cargo test --workspace --all-features` (41 wasm tests including a new copy→paste round-trip test), a new `clipboard.test.mjs` (7 `node --test` cases for the pure HTML/marker functions), and two new Playwright specs (`clipboard-rich.spec.mjs`, `ime-preedit.spec.mjs` — synthetic `ClipboardEvent`/`CompositionEvent` dispatch, matching the existing P1G-CLIPBOARD-001/P1G-IME-001 technique) exercising bold-copy round-trip, external-HTML paste, internal-marker paste, and preedit show/hide — all 10 e2e specs (8 prior + 2 new) stable across repeated runs. Manual paste from a real Word/Google Docs document remains an outstanding check outside automated coverage. |
| P1G-STRUCTURAL-DELETE-001 | Cross-structure delete: table-boundary crossing + visible edit failures | Claude Code | In review | Doc 67 P0 "Cross-structure delete/selection," fixing a user-reported bug ("multi-page copy and delete not working"). Root cause: `selection_delete_ops` (`casual-doc-wasm`) built a `JoinParagraphs` for every paragraph in a multi-paragraph delete range indiscriminately; `join_paragraphs` (`casual-doc-edit`) correctly rejects joining a body paragraph with a table-cell paragraph (they're never siblings in the same `Vec<BlockNode>`), throwing `EditError::Unsupported` — and the JS host's `runEdit` swallowed that as a bare `console.warn`, so a selection crossing into a table on Delete/Cut/type-over/paste-over silently did nothing. Fix: a new `paragraph_container_map` (mirrors `join_paragraphs`'s own recursive same-vec search, minting a fresh container id per table cell/`Sdt`) lets `selection_delete_ops` skip `JoinParagraphs` across a container boundary while still clearing all covered text — content is never lost and a table/cell is never dissolved by a generic delete, matching Word's own conservative behavior; `delete_selection` now pins the caret at the selection start via `apply_action_caret` instead of trusting the last op's natural place. `replaceSelection`/`pasteRichRuns` share the same fixed helper, so type-over and paste-over a cross-table selection are fixed too. List-join was investigated and found already correct (the surviving paragraph keeps its own properties, matching Word) — pinned with a regression test, not changed. Hyperlink partial-selection deletion (starts inside a `Hyperlink`, ends outside it) remains a known, separate `Unsupported` case — deferred (needs a "split a Hyperlink wrapper" primitive) but no longer silent: `runEdit`'s catch now also calls `setStatus(...)` with a bounded, generic message instead of only warning to the console, closing the "looks like nothing happened" gap for every one of its call sites. Verified with `cargo fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -D warnings`/`cargo test --workspace --all-features` (43 wasm tests: the exact reported repro — select from body text through the fixture's nested table cells and delete — now succeeds with the table intact and one undo restoring everything, plus a same-container regression and a list-join regression), and two new Playwright specs (`cross-structure-delete.spec.mjs`) confirming the table-crossing delete succeeds with no console warning and the table survives, and that a still-unsupported case (a partial hyperlink cut, built via the rich-paste bridge) now shows a status-bar error instead of silence — all 12 e2e specs stable across repeated runs. |
| P1G-DEVELOPER-SITE | Developer landing page and indexed live-demo route | Codex | Done | Doc 66: the GitHub Pages root is a developer-first landing page; the editor remains at `editor.html`; and `?demo=1` opens a redistribution-safe repository fixture. Added technical SEO, canonical/social metadata, JSON-LD, `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml`, and `llms.txt`; refreshed the README/editor screenshot; and kept current DOCX support separate from future SDK, format, and PDF-export goals. Verified 2026-07-29 with the local Pages build, desktop/mobile browser smoke, automatic demo loading, zero console errors, and successful production workflow run 30403402189 at `https://opendoc.casualoffice.org/`. |
| P1G-TEST-INFRA-PORTS | Per-worktree Playwright port + rustdoc private-link guard | Claude | In review | Fixes the systemic shared-port e2e flakiness that cost multiple agents real debugging (see P1G-REVIEW-050's "private port to avoid a concurrent agent's server on 8099" workaround). `webapp/playwright.config.mjs` previously used a fixed `:8099` with `reuseExistingServer: true` locally, so concurrent agents running `npm run test:e2e` from different worktrees all targeted 8099 and Playwright *reused* whichever `serve.py` already held the port — silently serving a **stale bundle from another worktree**. Now the port is `Number(process.env.PW_PORT) || portFromCwd()`, where `portFromCwd()` is a SHA-256 hash of `process.cwd()` (the worktree's `webapp` dir, unique per worktree) mapped into 20000–59999, threaded into `baseURL`, the `serve.py` command, and the readiness URL so all three agree; `reuseExistingServer` is now `false`, so a run always starts and tears down its own server and never reuses a stale one (a vanishingly rare hash collision becomes a loud `EADDRINUSE`/"already used" failure, not a silent stale serve). CI already ran with reuse disabled (it sets `CI`) and runs one job at a time, so CI's `browser-smoke` (`npm run test:e2e`) is unchanged. Also adds a `CONTRIBUTING.md` note on the rustdoc `private_intra_doc_links` gotcha that broke the CI `docs` job (`RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings"`) on #248 and #266: don't use `` [`Type`] `` intra-doc links to private `serde` structs (e.g. `ReviewCommentSummaryJson`, `A11yBlockJson`) — use plain `` `Type` `` code spans. **No Rust changed.** Verified: `npm run test:unit` (23 passed), full `npm run test:e2e` (85 passed) on the derived port 25502, and two targeted proofs — a decoy server left on `:8099` is ignored (run passes on 25502, decoy untouched), and a squatter on the worktree's own port makes Playwright fail loud ("…:25502/ is already used") instead of serving stale. |
| P1G-DEVELOPER-SITE-VISUAL-REFRESH | Align the developer site with the editor's visual system | Codex | Done | Doc 66 refinement: replaces the disconnected dark-glass presentation with the editor-aligned light paper/canvas system, tighter developer-first hierarchy, ruler motifs, bounded capability surfaces, and deliberately dark engine/code panels. Refreshes the live-editor screenshot, shares the self-hosted Inter chrome face, and adds permanent route/preview/mobile-overflow Playwright coverage; claims, routes, SEO metadata, and runtime policy are unchanged. Verified with `webapp/build.sh`, 15 frontend unit tests, and the complete 29-test Playwright suite. |
| P1G-DEVELOPER-SITE-FIDELITY-MATRIX | Public per-construct DOCX fidelity support-matrix page | Claude | In review (owner sign-off pending) | New `webapp/fidelity.html` renders an honest, data-grounded support matrix — one row per DOCX construct family (19), one column per stage (**Modeled · Rendered · Editable · Round-trips**) — from a single-source-of-truth data array in `webapp/src/fidelity.js`, styled with the marketing design system (`webapp/src/marketing.css`) as a real semantic `<table>` with `<th scope>` headers and a five-symbol legend (● full · ◐ partial · ▢ placeholder · ⊟ preserved-not-rendered · ○ not yet). Every classification is grounded in docs/18, docs/14 (P1A/P1F/P1G rows), docs/35, and the docs/55/60 fidelity audits, and is deliberately conservative: **images are not editable** (no insert/edit surface), **headers/footers/footnotes/text-boxes/fields are render-only** (not editing surfaces), **math is a placeholder** (opaque node, not typeset), **charts/SmartArt are preserved-not-rendered** (preview/text placeholder only), non-PNG/JPEG images/EMF-WMF/`w:altChunk` are visible placeholders, and comments/tracked-changes are explicitly labeled "not Word/Docs parity". Linked from the site nav; a "draft for owner review" banner flags that the claims should be reviewed before the page is prominently featured. Also references docs/18. **No Rust/WASM change.** Verified with a new `tests/fidelity_data.test.mjs` drift/honesty guard (locks the 19-family list, valid stage vocabulary, and load-bearing "must not overstate" cells — e.g. images not editable, math placeholder, charts preserved) in `npm run test:unit` (26 passed), a new `tests/e2e/fidelity-page.spec.mjs` asserting the accessible table renders with 19 row-headers and the honest cells, `webapp/build.sh`, and the full Playwright suite (83 passed). Owner to review the classifications before featuring. |

| P1F-FLOAT-CROSS-PARAGRAPH | Page/margin side-wrap exclusions across paragraphs | Codex | Done | Doc 65 Slice C: page/margin/column-relative `square`/`tight`/`through` pictures, text boxes, and groups produce rectangular page-local exclusions for top-level body paragraphs whose origins fall inside the float band, including paragraphs before a later page-relative anchor. A three-pass fixed point terminates with the widest/longest observed edge envelope; multiple edge floats union by maximum occupied interval. Full and cached entry points converge identically. Import and semantic export form a fixed point; generic, malformed, and out-of-domain frames remain reported rather than being misrepresented. A one-character `around`/`auto` frame plus its following paragraph flows as one coupled unit: the large initial keeps natural, unclipped ink at zero block advance, the next paragraph receives a measured leading exclusion, and full width returns below it; cached layout deliberately uses the same fresh coupled path. `margin` shifts the initial into the leading margin. Other wraps/multi-character/non-adjacent frames remain ordinary flow; modeled anchor/alignment/position values are semantic-only in this first layout increment. ## Application Shell & Editor (Phase 1G)

The interactive layer: the on-screen document surface, the read-only viewer
shipped first, and the editor it grows into. Architecture in
`docs/56-EDITOR-SHELL-AND-RENDER-ARCHITECTURE.md`. Decision: **OnlyOffice-style
fat client** — model + layout + paint run client-side on canvas, interaction is
custom — realized as **Rust → WASM** (not hand-written JS). The viewer is built on
the editor architecture so no viewer work is throwaway. Edit path realizes the
doc 45 seams (I1 choke point, I2 closed op set, I3 `NodeId`/`ModelPos`, I4
sidecar) via `casual-doc-transaction`. Decision in
`docs/56-EDITOR-SHELL-AND-RENDER-ARCHITECTURE.md`; build-level plan (crates, the
`casual-doc-wasm` API, the JS↔WASM data contract, milestones, gates) in
`docs/57-PHASE-1G-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`.

| ID | Title | Owner | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P1G-OBJ-DESIGN | Drawing-object + header/footer editing design | Claude Code | Designing | `docs/85-DRAWING-OBJECT-AND-HEADER-FOOTER-EDITING-DESIGN.md`: next-phase design for making drawing objects editable (Phase A) then headers/footers (Phase B), in that order because real-world headers/footers are predominantly images + text boxes, so B depends on A. Design-first per AGENTS.md — this row (and the `P1G-OBJ-*`/`P1G-HF-*` rows below) is a design gate, not implementation. The doc grounds every op against the actual model (`InlineNode::Drawing`/`AnchoredDrawing`/`TextBox`/`Group`, `Definitions.headers/footers`, `DrawingAnchor`) and the live `casual_doc_edit::Operation` set (which is body-only and has no object/header ops today), extends doc 58's declared `HitTarget`/`Selection::Object`/`Handle` arms, and defines a universal object interaction grammar (click→context bar; Enter/double-click→edit; Escape→object, Escape again→text caret). Blocked on owner decisions in §9 (image-crop + inline alt-text model additions; "basic shapes" scope; object edits under Suggesting mode; `title_page`/section-running op shape; link-to-previous semantics; host-provides-image-bytes contract). |
| P1G-OBJ-SELECT | Object selection + hit-testing (`Selection::Object`, handles) | Claude Code | Done | Phase A / Slice 2. Ships doc 58's inline-object `HitTarget` + `Selection::Object` + engine-drawn selection chrome, read-only (no mutation ops). **`casual-doc-wasm`:** `objectAt(page,x,y)`, `objectRect(node)`, `objectHandles(node)` (docs/85 §3.3) resolve a page-local point to a selectable inline object and answer its placed rect + eight resize/move handle centers (NW,N,NE,E,SE,S,SW,W). They walk the paginated layout (public `Page`/`PlacedFragment`/`BlockFragment` fields), mirror composition's inline-object placement (`content_origin + box.origin`), and correlate each placed box back to its model drawing/text-box node (image by media part-name then order; text box by order). Scope: inline images (`w:drawing` pictures) and inline text boxes in top-level body paragraphs. **`webapp`:** a new `objectSelection` state (`{node, kind, mode}`) painted as an accent outline + eight grip divs on the same overlay as the caret/selection (zero drift), plus a placeholder context bar (docs/85 §4.1). **Grammar (§4, `P1G-OBJ-GRAMMAR` interaction half):** single-click selects the object (handles + context bar); double-click / Enter enters a container's edit mode (leaf image → context-action placeholder); Escape is the two-step exit (editing → selected → surrounding-text caret); clicking body text or navigating the caret deselects; a selected object swallows text keys and Delete reports "later slice" (no stale-caret edit). Verified with a `casual-doc-wasm` regression (image resolve + rect round-trip + 8 handles + miss/unknown-node), `cargo fmt`/strict Clippy/`cargo test --workspace`, 23 frontend unit tests, `webapp/build.sh`, and a new `object-selection.spec.mjs` (4 specs: click→select+8 handles; Escape→text caret; click-text deselect; text-keys/Delete swallowed) plus the full 84-test browser suite. **Explicitly deferred to later slices:** all move/resize/insert/delete mutation (next: `P1G-OBJ-GEOMETRY`) — handles are visible but not yet draggable; anchored/floating objects, table-cell and header/footer objects, handle-grip hit resolution, in-text-box caret editing, and the real `editorCommands(object)` context bar. |
| P1G-OBJ-GRAMMAR | Universal object interaction grammar + context bar | — | Not started | Phase A. §4 grammar for images/text-boxes/shapes/groups/tables via doc 84 `editorCommands(object)`. Gated by design. |
| P1G-OBJ-ANCHOR-SELECT | Floating-object selection + move (`SetAnchor`) + wrap | Claude Code | Done | Phase A / Slice 3b, unblocking what `P1G-OBJ-GEOMETRY` deferred: **floating/anchored objects are now selectable, movable, wrappable, and resizable.** **Identity (`casual-doc-layout`):** `PlacedAnchor` gains a `node: Option<NodeId>` back to its model anchor, set in `anchor.rs` at each construction site (`Some` for a top-level `AnchoredDrawing`/floating `TextBox`; `None` for group children, not yet individually selectable). **Selection (`casual-doc-wasm`):** `object_boxes` also walks `page.anchored` (whose `rect` is already the absolute page rect), filtered to body floats (a header/footer float's node isn't in `body()`); `objectAt`/`objectRect`/`objectHandles` therefore resolve floats, and `ObjectHitPayload.anchored` tells the host a float is movable/wrappable. **Move + wrap (`casual-doc-edit`):** new additive `SetAnchor { object, anchor: Box<DrawingAnchor> }` op (one op covers position + wrap + wrap-distances + z-order), self-inverse carrying the previous anchor, resolving `AnchoredDrawing`/floating-`TextBox`/anchored-`Group` targets. `SetExtent` (from `P1G-OBJ-GEOMETRY`) now also resizes `AnchoredDrawing`/floating `TextBox`. **`casual-doc-wasm`:** `setObjectAnchorPosition(node, leftEmu, topEmu)` (free drag → `page`-relative `posOffset`s, one `HistoryKind::ObjectMove` action), `setObjectWrap(node, mode)` (`square`/`tight`/`through`/`topAndBottom`/`behind`/`front`), `objectWrap(node)`. **`webapp`:** dragging a selected float's body previews an outline and commits one `SetAnchor` on release (a bare click commits nothing); the context bar exposes a live Wrap segmented control for floats; move/wrap are blocked fail-closed in Suggesting/Viewing. Verified with a `casual-doc-edit` regression (`SetAnchor` move+re-wrap + exact inverse + inline-reject), a `casual-doc-wasm` regression (float select + move+undo + wrap reflect + resize + **DOCX export→reopen round-trip** of move/wrap/resize), `cargo fmt`/strict Clippy/`cargo test --workspace`, 26 frontend unit tests, `webapp/build.sh`, a generated `webapp/float.docx` fixture (via an `--ignored` engine test — no shipped sample doc contains a float), and a new `object-anchor.spec.mjs` (4 specs: float select + controls; body-drag move + one undo; wrap-mode change reflects; float resize). **Still deferred:** group children and header/footer floats (selection), object insert/delete (`P1G-OBJ-STRUCTURE`), image content ops, and inline↔floating conversion. |
| P1G-OBJ-GEOMETRY | Move / resize / wrap / z-order ops | Claude Code | Done | Phase A / Slice 3, building on `P1G-OBJ-SELECT`: makes the selection handles functional for **resize** (move/wrap for floats followed in `P1G-OBJ-ANCHOR-SELECT`). **`casual-doc-edit`:** new additive `SetExtent { object, extent: Option<Extent> }` op through the `apply` choke point — self-inverse carrying the previous extent (retained-value pattern), resolving the target via a recursive `find_object_extent_mut` across body paragraphs (through hyperlink/revision wrappers), table cells, block SDTs, and inline text-box bodies. **`casual-doc-wasm`:** `setObjectExtent(node, wEmu, hEmu)` (one undoable `HistoryKind::ObjectResize` action) + `objectExtent(node)` (authored EMU, or the placed size when unauthored). **`webapp`:** the eight grips from Slice 2 are now draggable — a pointerdown on a grip starts a resize (the grip div *is* the `Float { handle }` hit resolution #269 deferred, drawn from engine geometry), previews as a dashed outline (host chrome, no per-frame ops), and commits **one** `SetExtent` on release (EMU from the final placed twips); corner grips resize both axes with Shift constraining aspect (docs/85 §10.3), edge grips one axis; Escape/pointer-cancel/blur abort the drag. **Mode-gated fail-closed:** blocked in Suggesting (not a tracked revision) and Viewing (read-only). Verified with a `casual-doc-edit` regression (`SetExtent` apply + inverse round-trip + unknown-node reject), a `casual-doc-wasm` regression (resize + one-undo + **DOCX export→reopen round-trip** of the new extent), `cargo fmt`/strict Clippy/`cargo test --workspace`, 23 frontend unit tests, `webapp/build.sh`, and a new `object-geometry.spec.mjs` (3 specs: corner drag resizes + one undo reverts; edge drag is single-axis; Suggesting-mode drag is blocked with the size unchanged). **Explicitly deferred:** **move** and **wrap-mode / z-order** are floating-object ops (they mutate `DrawingAnchor`, which inline objects lack) — they need anchored/floating-object *selection* first (`PlacedAnchor` node identity, the `P1G-OBJ-SELECT` follow-up), so `SetAnchor` and wrap/z-order ship in that slice. Insert/delete objects (`P1G-OBJ-STRUCTURE`) and image content ops remain separate. |

All fields are additive `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]`. **Import** (`casual-doc-import`): parses `a:srcRect@l/t/r/b` inside any `pic:blipFill` (routed to the lone/inline/anchored picture or the open group `pic:pic`), clamps and drops an all-zero (identity) crop; the inline `wp:docPr@descr` now models onto the inline `Drawing` (was previously reported as unmodeled). Threaded through the text-box content-frame save/restore. **Export** (`casual-doc-export`): a shared `write_src_rect` emits `a:srcRect` (non-zero edges only) inside `pic:blipFill` for inline/anchored/group pictures; the inline `wp:docPr` re-emits `descr`. **Layout+render** (`casual-doc-layout`/`casual-doc-render`): crop threads `Drawing`→`FlowItem::Image`→`InlineImageSpec`/`InlineImage`→`PaintItem::Image` (and `AnchoredDrawing`/`GroupPicture`→`AnchorContent::Image`→`PaintItem::Image`), folded into the paragraph galley-cache hash; the CPU raster backend extracts the visible source sub-rectangle (`Pixmap::clone_rect`) and scales it into the display box, so a cropped image renders cropped — the display box is unchanged (`a:srcRect` selects source pixels, not the box). `casual-doc-model` moved from a dev- to a normal dependency of `casual-doc-render`. **Explicitly out of scope (later slices):** `SetImageCrop`/`SetObjectDescr` edit ops, selection/handles, and any object-editing UI. Object selection no longer advertises deferred features: image feedback is transient and clears on Escape, caret navigation, or body interaction, while the context bar describes only functional resize behavior. JS/CSS/HTML/docs/tests only; command IDs and transaction paths remain unchanged. Synced to `main` at `70b303a`. Verified with `node --check webapp/src/main.js`, `git diff --check`, 26/26 frontend unit tests, 11/11 final focused ribbon/object Playwright tests, responsive measurement/screenshots at 1440/1280/1100/900/760/480/360px (all seven Home groups inline at 1280px; no document-width overflow), and the full Chromium suite at 115/117. The remaining two `checklist.spec.mjs` failures reproduce unchanged from the pre-change `HEAD` against the same generated WASM artifact (checklist creation reflects as an ordinary bullet), so they are a pre-existing engine/artifact baseline failure rather than a ribbon-correction regression. |

| P1G-001 | WASM bridge + first pixel | Claude Code | In review | New `casual-doc-wasm` (`wasm-bindgen` façade, `crate-type=["cdylib","rlib"]`): `open(bytes)` (import→paginate), `pageCount` getter, `pageSize(i)` (per-section geometry), `renderPage(i,dpi)→PageBitmap{widthPx,heightPx,rgba:Uint8ClampedArray}` — the same `import_package`→`paginate_document`→`compose_page`→`render` path the native `render_gallery` uses, serving faces from the post-pagination shaper registry. Fallible logic sits in `_inner` methods returning `Result<_,String>` so the `#[wasm_bindgen]` boundary (which converts to a thrown JS `Error`, `to_js`) is the only JS-touching code and native `cargo test` exercises the engine directly. Browser harness lives in **`webapp/`** (drop/upload a `.docx` → blit every page to `<canvas>` at 96·zoom·dpr; drag-drop, zoom, HiDPI-crisp), built by `webapp/build.sh` (wasm-pack `--target web`) and deployed to **GitHub Pages** by `.github/workflows/pages.yml` — an explicit **developer test harness only** (see `webapp/README.md`), the browser-first surface for finding/fine-tuning fidelity. 3 native tests (page-count parity vs native; every page renders with consistent dimensions + `w·h·4` RGBA; out-of-range is an error not a panic). `wasm-opt` given `--enable-bulk-memory`/`sign-ext`/`nontrapping-fptoint`. All gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy, test, doc, wasm32 check, deny). Structured `SdkError` code/severity + import compat report on errors (§5.5) deferred; §4.4–4.6 (text layer/hit-test/editing) is P1G-002+. |
| P1G-001a | Web font provisioning (CJK / complex-script fallback) | Claude Code | In review | Browser build ships only bundled Latin faces, so CJK/complex-script runs rendered as blank/`.notdef` tofu. Exposes the existing `FontRegistry` seam across WASM — `missingCoverage()` (u32 code points that shaped to `.notdef`) + `registerFallbackFont(bytes, scripts[])` / `registerFont(bytes)` (register → `repaginate`). `webapp/` inspects `missingCoverage()`, buckets code points by range → the needed Noto face(s) (CJK region OTFs from jsDelivr googlefonts/noto-cjk; Arabic/Devanagari/Hebrew/Thai from notofonts), `fetch`es (cached), registers as script fallbacks, re-renders. Verified end-to-end via the WASM methods with a real Noto JP face: `日本語` renders where it was blank (before/after rasters). Note: `missingCoverage` accumulates within one open document (never resets across re-pagination) — provisioning runs once on open, each `open()` builds a fresh shaper, so this is correct. Known gaps: color emoji (separate font case) and Japanese line-breaking (parley has no `ja` segmentation dictionary — "No segmentation model for ja"). All gates green. |

Frontend (`webapp/`): pointer drag → `hitTest` both ends → a `Selection` → caret/highlight **we draw** from engine geometry over a per-page overlay, so it matches the raster **exactly** (verified in-browser: highlight hugs the glyphs, no drift); ⌘C → `copyText` → clipboard (verified: "Paragraph with an image:" → 24 chars). `copyText` walks the model in document order (body + tables + block SDTs) slicing each node's shaped text; the byte space comes from a new authoritative `casual_doc_layout::flow::node_plain_text` mirroring `collect_runs` exactly (Run/Tab/Symbol + Hyperlink/Revision/Sdt recursion). Anchored on `NodeId`/`ModelPos` (I3). 6 wasm tests (copy round-trip incl. backward + cross-paragraph, caret/selection geometry, hit→node, malformed-id safe). **Deferred (additive per doc 58):** transparent text layer for native find/AT, `linkAt` hyperlink-open, byte-exactness where fields/inline-objects contribute glyphs, and the object/table `HitTarget`/`Selection` variants for editing. All gates green. |

Per doc 59 (decision), new **`casual-doc-edit`** crate is the choke point (I1): `apply(&mut v1::Document, ids, Operation) -> inverse` over a closed set (I2) — `InsertText`/`DeleteText` on top-level runs, byte-offset anchor space shared with hit-testing, per-op inverses for undo, validates-before-mutates (no partial edits). `Document::body_mut()` added (additive). New popover (Title/Author/Subject/Category/Keywords/Comments) opened from a header button, Apply/Cancel. **Page setup:** new `Operation::SetSectionGeometry { section, page_size, page_margins, orientation }` — same snapshot/validate/rollback pattern, targets one section by `SectionId`. WASM `pageSetup()`/`setPageSetup(json)`, also JSON-bridged (`PageSetupJson`) to avoid the same many-args problem; caught and fixed a real name collision (`casual_doc_model::v1::PageSize` vs. an unrelated pre-existing local wasm-bindgen `PageSize`) via an import alias. All three ops route through the existing `apply`/`apply_action_caret` choke point and are undoable. **Found and fixed in passing:** `webapp/demo.docx` (a gitignored build artifact) had been silently overwritten with `sample.docx`'s bytes at some point outside `build.sh` (source fixture `fixtures/corpus/real-producer-rich.docx` was untouched) — caused 4 unrelated e2e specs to fail against the wrong document; re-running the copy step fixed it, root cause not identified (not `build.sh`, which is correct) but blast radius is contained to a disposable, regeneratable artifact. 6 new Rust unit tests (2 core-properties, 3 section-geometry, covering apply/inverse/oversized-rejection/unknown-section) + 5 new Playwright specs (`document-properties.spec.mjs`: rename incl. extension/blank edge cases, properties apply+undo+cancel, page-setup apply+undo+orientation-swap). All 20 e2e specs + 13 unit tests + full `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` + `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings` + `cargo +1.96.0 fmt` green. |

| P1G-FIND-REPLACE-002 | Find/replace panel — fix unstyled buttons, add Replace all, accurate match count, ribbon entry point | Claude Code | In review | Owner: "the UI of find and replace is not good." Root cause: the prev/next/close buttons referenced CSS class `icon-btn`, which had **zero rules anywhere in the stylesheet** — they rendered as unstyled native buttons inside an otherwise fully-themed panel; the panel was an 8-column single-row grid; there was no Replace-all; the status always read a hardcoded `"1 match"` regardless of the real count; there was no toolbar entry point (Cmd+F/palette only). Fixed: buttons now use the real `.btn.icon-only` class; panel restructured into two rows (find controls / replace controls) as a proper card; new `#replaceAll` button (`replaceAllMatches` — repeatedly finds-and-replaces from the top, resuming each search just past the just-inserted replacement so a replacement containing the query, e.g. "cat"→"cats", can't self-match and loop; bounded by `FIND_SCAN_CAP`); new `scanAllMatches` walks every match in document order (deduping against every key seen so far, not just the first — the engine's wrap fallback can resurface a later match instead of cycling cleanly back to the first one) to drive an accurate `"x of y"` / `"N matches"` status, while the 0/1-match cases keep the exact `"No match"`/`"1 match"` strings several other specs already assert on. New ribbon **Editing** group (Home tab) with a Find button (Material-Symbols-style inlined SVG, matching the existing B/I/U icon set — no new icon dependency). New `tests/e2e/find-replace.spec.mjs` (ribbon entry point, accurate multi-match counting, replace-all + undo); updated `clipboard-rich.spec.mjs`'s stale `"1 match"` assertion, which only ever passed because of the hardcoded-count bug it was inadvertently relying on — the marker genuinely occurs twice there. **Follow-up filed, not fixed here:** `findText`'s forward-wrap fallback cannot relocate a match when the search start position sits *inside* that match's own span (only reachable today via undo re-parking the caret there) — a narrow pre-existing engine limitation, out of scope for a UI fix; worth a small dedicated `casual-doc-wasm` fix later. All 15 webapp e2e specs + 10 unit tests green; no Rust changes. |

| P1G-028 | Paragraph — **complete** (options menu, numeric indents, borders, tab stops) + pageBreakBefore paginator fix | Claude Code | In review | The completing paragraph slice — one PR taking paragraph editing to Word-grade. Verified in-browser: applied 1.5 + 12pt-after and Double + 24pt-before to real paragraphs (model reads back `percent=150/rule=auto`, `after=240tw`; `rule=0` confirms the fidelity fix), table cells untouched, two undos restore the original heading spacing. New wasm test `paragraph_spacing_applies_reflects_and_undoes`. Gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy `--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked`, wasm test). **Next paragraph slices:** shading wire-up (`setParagraphShading` exists, unbound in UI), pagination flags (keep-next / page-break-before / widow), numeric indent + first-line UI, borders / tab stops. |
| P1G-026 | Horizontal ruler (display → draggable, table-safe) | Claude Code | In review | A Word/Docs-style horizontal ruler above the page. WASM: `pageGeometry()` (page width + side margins, twips) and `paragraphIndent(node)` (start/end/first-line/hanging). `webapp/`: a sticky ruler strip sized to the page width per zoom — a lighter content span between shaded margin zones, hairline inch ticks with numbers, and three accent **pentagon** indent markers (first-line ▼, left ▲, right ▲) that reflect the caret paragraph's indentation. Restyled twice for polish (bare triangles → pentagons; tightened the toolbar→ruler→page spacing via a negative margin that cancels the page-flex gap). **The ruler is `pointer-events: none` and display-only** — an earlier draggable-marker version used `setPointerCapture`, which, when a click didn't release the capture, captured the pointer document-wide and froze the page; removing the drag and passing all pointer events through eliminates that failure mode entirely. Absolute indent setters (`setLeftIndent`/`setRightIndent`/`setFirstLineIndent`) are in the WASM API. Verified in-browser (light): clean, tight, aligned to the page. **Follow-up landed (PR #194):** the freeze was root-caused to a *different* bug — the ruler is prepended into `#pages`, and `pageFromEvent` indexed page clicks by child position, so every click resolved to the wrong (or a missing) page and died (looked frozen). Fixed by indexing among `.page-wrap` only. With that fixed, **draggable indent markers were restored safely** — only the markers are pointer-interactive (strip stays click-through), drag uses window `pointermove`/`pointerup` (never `setPointerCapture`). Indent drags are **table-safe**: `apply_indent_props` skips paragraphs inside table cells (a body-scale indent overflowed narrow cells and mangled the table). **Deferred:** draggable page margins; tab stops; the vertical ruler; a cell-relative in-table ruler. |
| P1G-025 | Tables — delete table + reflect/arm all run properties at the caret | Claude Code | In review | Two things. **(1) Delete table.** New ops `DeleteTable { table }` / `InsertTable { container, index, table }` (container = the body `None` or the owning cell/SDT id, so the inverse restores the table to its exact spot; refuses to empty a cell). WASM `deleteTable(node)` (innermost table; caret lands on the first body paragraph) and a **Delete table** item in the right-click menu. Verified: right-click → Delete table removes the whole table, caret to the heading, stats update (16w/8¶ → 10w/3¶); undo restores. **(2) "Font size / style not picked up on cursor."** The caret-inherited run style was queryable in the engine (`caret_run_style`) but never surfaced. Wired WASM `caretRunStyle`; the toolbar now reflects size / font / color / super-sub at a **collapsed caret** (not only over a selection), enables the run controls at a caret, and — matching the B/I/U/S toggles — **arms** font/size/color/highlight/super-sub into the pending format so the next typed text carries them (`insertStyledText` extended to a full `FormatDelta`). Verified: clicking a heading shows Style=Heading 1 with the run controls enabled; arming 24pt shows 24 in the Size box and the engine test confirms typed text carries the armed size. Gates green (fmt 1.96.0, clippy `--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked`, workspace test, wasm). |

| P1G-022 | Premium UI pass — toolbar redesign, active-cell highlight, status footer | Claude Code | In review | First-impression polish (owner: "even if it's testing, everyone sees it; first impression is everlasting") + the table-selection feedback the Google-Docs research called for. **Active-cell highlight:** `LayoutSnapshot::cell_rect(node)` returns the innermost cell's page-local border box (`CellFragment.x`/`width`, row/merge height; nested-cell aware); WASM `cellRect`; `webapp/` draws a soft accent ring around the cell the caret is in — verified in-browser (orange ring on "Nested A"). **Contextual table controls:** the four row/column buttons moved into a `Table` group that is **hidden unless the caret is in a table** (no permanent clutter for prose docs) — the first step of the Google-Docs-modeled plan; the right-click menu is the next. **Status footer:** WASM `documentStats() -> {words, paragraphs, pages}`; the footer now shows the filename (left) and **`N words · N paragraphs · Page X of Y`** (right, live on edits + caret moves), moving status out of the header. **Toolbar/chrome redesign** (`index.html` + rewritten `style.css`): refined graphite+orange palette (light/dark), a real button system (primary Open, icon Save, zoom −/＋ stepper), custom-chevron selects, accent-tinted active toggles, a proper drop-zone card, layered page shadow, and a slimmer 30px control grid.
